# Merge candidates (phase 1, deterministic)

Groups of concept ids that are probably the same concept. Nothing has been merged.
Rules: (1) identical after singular/plural folding or hyphen removal; (2) matches an alias
of a canon spine name (full name, name without parentheses, parenthetical, parts split on
/ & , vs.); (3) Levenshtein distance ≤ 2, both ids ≥ 8 chars; (4) one id contained in
another as a whole-token run, sharing a field. Each id: number of digests, fields, one example definition.
Regenerated by scripts/build_kg.py.

## 1. Plural / hyphen variants (51 groups)

- group: entropy-h-x = entropy-hx
  - `entropy-h-x` — 5 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[gibson-2019-efficiency/main]]: average surprisal of a random variable, as uncertainty about its value
  - `entropy-hx` — 15 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: $-\sum p(x)\log_2 p(x)$; average bits needed to describe $X$
- group: fano-inequality = fanos-inequality
  - `fano-inequality` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[polyanskiy-2025-information/ch19-error]]: gives the same bound in one line
  - `fanos-inequality` — 16 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch02-entropy]]: $H(P_e)+P_e\log(|\mathcal X|-1)\ge H(X|Y)$
- group: sufficient-statistic = sufficient-statistics
  - `sufficient-statistic` — 15 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[bertsekas-2026-rlcourse/ch01-exact-p4]]: quantity summarizing all essential content of the available information for control; $I_k$ or $b_k$
  - `sufficient-statistics` — 2 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch07-central-p1]]: data summaries that alone enter the likelihood; with Gaussian errors, $\bar y$ and $s^2$
- group: bayesian-occam-razor = bayesian-occams-razor
  - `bayesian-occam-razor` — 2 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch24-exact]]: the Occam factor in (24.13), a volume factor from integrating out μ
  - `bayesian-occams-razor` — 14 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch22-finite]]: the "intrinsic Bayes penalty": marginal likelihood tends to fall as items are spread across more components
- group: arrow-impossibility-theorem = arrows-impossibility-theorem
  - `arrow-impossibility-theorem` — 5 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch11-groups]]: cited as illuminating the folk theory's internal contradictions
  - `arrows-impossibility-theorem` — 7 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: any process meeting certain reasonable conditions faces similar difficulties as majority rule
- group: cox-theorem = coxs-theorem
  - `cox-theorem` — 9 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/appa-other]]: consistency forces the sum and product rules once plausibilities are real numbers
  - `coxs-theorem` — 3 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch15-paradoxes]]: the finite-set derivation of the rules; Jaynes accepts well-defined limits
- group: nuisance-parameter = nuisance-parameters
  - `nuisance-parameter` — 9 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch03-introduction]]: a parameter needed for the model but not of inferential interest, e.g. the error scale
  - `nuisance-parameters` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch15-outcome]]: parameters not of primary interest, e.g. those of the outcome model or the treatment model
- group: prisoner-dilemma = prisoners-dilemma
  - `prisoner-dilemma` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[mitchell-2009-guidedtour/ch14-prospects]]: two-player game where self-interest gives both a worse payoff than cooperation
  - `prisoners-dilemma` — 9 digest(s); decision-game-theory, political-science — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: two-player game with payoff order $T>R>P>S$ and $R>(T+S)/2$; defection is dominant but mutual defection is worse than mutual cooperation
- group: propositional-attitude = propositional-attitudes
  - `propositional-attitude` — 9 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: a belief is an attitude to a content; adults' content is propositional
  - `propositional-attitudes` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[wittgenstein-1922-tractatus/ch00-introduction]]: believing, doubting, desiring, etc., covered by the problem of the logical form of belief; Russell analyses belief as a relation between …
- group: lagrange-multiplier = lagrange-multipliers
  - `lagrange-multiplier` — 4 digest(s); control-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch19-constrained]]: the vector $z$ in $L(x,z)=\|f\|^2+g^Tz$
  - `lagrange-multipliers` — 3 digest(s); control-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/app-appendices]]: Lagrangian $L(x,z)=h(x)+g(x)^Tz$ for equality constraints
- group: bellman-equation = bellman-equations
  - `bellman-equation` — 5 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch10-x]]: here the functional equation $f=\max_G\min_{G'}\iint[R+h\,f(T,T')]$
  - `bellman-equations` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch19-reinforcement]]: self-consistency relations linking values at $t$ and $t+1$
- group: successive-approximation = successive-approximations
  - `successive-approximation` — 2 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch10-x]]: $f_{n+1}=\max\min T(f_n)$; converges to the unique solution
  - `successive-approximations` — 4 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch01-i-p1]]: iterate $f_{N+1}=\max_yT(f_N,y)$; converges under Theorem 1's conditions
- group: econ-vs-human = econs-vs-humans
  - `econ-vs-human` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch01-supposedly]]: Econ: a fictional perfect optimizer with unbiased beliefs; Human: a real person who departs from it
  - `econs-vs-humans` — 4 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch34-frames]]: Econs have reality-bound preferences; Humans' are frame-bound

  - `supposedly-irrelevant-factor` — 3 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch03-list]]: a factor that theory says should not affect choice but does (SIF)

  - `iterated-prisoner-dilemma` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch02-success]]: the Prisoner's Dilemma played over many moves, with each move's choice able to depend on history
  - `iterated-prisoners-dilemma` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch05-evolution]]: repeated PD where players recognise past partners and may condition on history

- group: over-dispersion = overdispersion

  - `bias-variance-tradeoff` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch11-model]]: protection against misspecification bias costs wider confidence intervals
- group: chaos-theorem = chaos-theorems
  - `chaos-theorem` — 2 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch11-problems-p1]]: with two or more dimensions and three or more sincere voters, there is typically no Condorcet winner
  - `chaos-theorems` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: results showing majority-dominant policies in multiple dimensions are fragile

  - `convergence-diagnostics-r-hat-ess-divergences` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2020-workflow/main-p1]]: $\widehat R<1.01$, $R^*$, effective sample size, absence of divergent transitions; they cannot detect a model coded differently than inte…
  - `convergence-diagnostics-rhat-ess-divergences` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch08-markov]]: here `n_eff` (effective sample count) and Rhat (Gelman–Rubin). Rhat may be seen "perhaps as a signal of danger, but never of safety"
- group: necessary-sufficient-condition = necessary-sufficient-conditions
  - `necessary-sufficient-condition` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch02-science]]: necessary: absence rules the outcome out; sufficient: presence guarantees it

- group: private-vs-common-value = private-vs-common-values
  - `private-vs-common-value` — 2 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch10-auctions]]: whether your value depends on others' estimates
  - `private-vs-common-values` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch09-bayesian]]: valuation depends only on one's own signal vs also on others' signals
- group: sigma-point = sigma-points
  - `sigma-point` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch01-symbols]]: points such as $\mathcal X^{(i)}$, with weights $W_i$
  - `sigma-points` — 2 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch08-general]]: evaluation points of a numerical integration rule, used to propagate a Gaussian
- group: sigma-point-method = sigma-point-methods
  - `sigma-point-method` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch09-gaussian]]: numerical integration with unit points $\boldsymbol\xi^{(i)}$, weights $W_i$
  - `sigma-point-methods` — 2 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch17-epilogue]]: unscented, spherical cubature, Gauss–Hermite and higher-order rules for Gaussian filters
- group: signature-limit = signature-limits
  - `signature-limit` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2011-mindreaders/ch07-elaborating]]: a fixed limit on a module's processing (e.g. 3–4 items; Level-1 only) that marks its influence and does not follow from general resources
  - `signature-limits` — 2 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: arbitrary, domain-specific limits (e.g. 3 items, ratio) used to identify an efficient system
- group: warm-up = warmup
  - `warm-up` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[betancourt-2017-hmc/main-p1]]: discarding initial converging samples; can also tune the transition

  - `competing-event` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch08-selection]]: event that prevents the outcome of interest, such as death
  - `competing-events` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch17-causal]]: events (e.g., other-cause death) that prevent the event of interest; no handling is fully satisfactory
- group: default-option = default-options
  - `default-option` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch32-keeping]]: the "normal" choice; departures from it produce more regret and blame
  - `default-options` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch32-going]]: what happens if a person does nothing; powerful in organ donation
- group: duverger-law = duvergers-law
  - `duverger-law` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch14-social-p3]]: SMDP systems encourage two-party systems
  - `duvergers-law` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch07-voting]]: FPP associated with two parties, PR with multiple parties
- group: efficiency-wage = efficiency-wages
  - `efficiency-wage` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch13-incentives]]: wage above the outside option, paid so long as no shirking is found, so that cheating is not worth it
  - `efficiency-wages` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[stiglitz-2002-paradigm/main-p2]]: it pays firms to pay more than they must, e.g. to reduce labor turnover costs
- group: evidence-decibel = evidence-decibels
  - `evidence-decibel` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch05-queer]]: evidence $=10\log_{10}$(odds), in db; used for −100 db, 41 db, 30 db, −70 db
  - `evidence-decibels` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch04-elementary]]: $e=10\log_{10}$(odds), in db; posterior = prior + log likelihood ratio
- group: flexibility-efficiency-trade-off = flexibility-efficiency-tradeoff
  - `flexibility-efficiency-trade-off` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2011-mindreaders/coda]]: mindreading must be both flexible and fast; the demands compete
  - `flexibility-efficiency-tradeoff` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2011-mindreaders/ch01-introduction]]: these two properties tend to be negatively correlated in cognitive systems
- group: free-floating-rationale = free-floating-rationales
  - `free-floating-rationale` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch08-evolution]]: a rationale for a design not represented by any agent
  - `free-floating-rationales` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch07-intentional]]: reasons a design has that no individual represents; evolution “chose” it for them
- group: group-norm = group-norms
  - `group-norm` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[katz-1955-personalinfluence/ch04-norms]]: a shared standard or frame of reference that emerges from interaction
  - `group-norms` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[katz-1955-personalinfluence/ch03-essay]]: the authors' label for opinions and attitudes shared person-to-person within small groups
- group: hidden-unit = hidden-units
  - `hidden-unit` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch03-shallow]]: activation $h_d=a[\theta_{d0}+\sum_i\theta_{di}x_i]$; also called neuron
  - `hidden-units` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch43-boltzmann]]: latent variables inducing higher-order visible correlations with pairwise weights
- group: indigenous-knowledge-system = indigenous-knowledge-systems
  - `indigenous-knowledge-system` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[rogers-2003-diffusion/ch02-history-p1]]: local knowledge that affects acceptance of innovations
  - `indigenous-knowledge-systems` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[rogers-2003-diffusion/ch06-attributes-p2]]: traditional knowledge that adopters use to give meaning to innovations
- group: missing-data-mechanism = missing-data-mechanisms
  - `missing-data-mechanism` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch18-models]]: the conditional law $p(I|y,\phi)$ of the inclusion indicator given the complete data
  - `missing-data-mechanisms` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch03-statistics-p3]]: MCAR, MAR, MNAR, defined via $p(\mathbf R_n|\mathbf X_n)$
- group: plott-theorem = plotts-theorem
  - `plott-theorem` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch05-spatial-p3]]: distance preferences, odd number of voters, radially symmetric ideal points make a voter's ideal point the equilibrium; in the example, $…
  - `plotts-theorem` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch05-spatial-p1]]: radial symmetry plus odd voters and distance-based preferences gives $W(x^*)=\emptyset$
- group: potential-falsifier = potential-falsifiers
  - `potential-falsifier` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch04-falsifiability]]: a basic statement that the theory prohibits
  - `potential-falsifiers` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch06-degrees]]: the class of basic statements a theory forbids
- group: request-vs-influence = requests-vs-influence
  - `request-vs-influence` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[katz-1955-personalinfluence/ch08-variations]]: a request makes the wife a purchasing agent; influence is advice or testimonial
  - `requests-vs-influence` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[katz-1955-personalinfluence/ch10-marketing]]: a request leaves the decision with the requester; influence leaves it with the wife
- group: response-effect = response-effects
  - `response-effect` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch05-making]]: seemingly irrelevant questionnaire features that change responses; for Zaller, not mere artifacts
  - `response-effects` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch02-information]]: systematic changes in answers from question order, context or trivial wording
- group: semi-gradient-method = semi-gradient-methods
  - `semi-gradient-method` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sutton-2018-rl/ch11-off]]: update with the gradient of the estimate only, ignoring the bootstrapped target's dependence on $\mathbf w$; not true SGD
  - `semi-gradient-methods` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sutton-2018-rl/ch09-policy-p1]]: bootstrapping updates that ignore the weights' effect on the target, so are not true gradient descent
- group: system-trap = system-traps
  - `system-trap` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[meadows-2008-thinking/ch05-system]]: an archetype seen as both a trap and an opportunity, since structure can be changed
  - `system-traps` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[meadows-2008-thinking/app-untitled]]: recurring structures such as policy resistance, with remedies
- group: two-degree-of-freedom = two-degrees-of-freedom
  - `two-degree-of-freedom` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch12-frequency]]: controller with feedback $C$ and feedforward $F$ designed separately
  - `two-degrees-of-freedom` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch02-feedback]]: controller with different transfer functions $C_{ur}$ and $C_{uy}$

## 2. Matches to canon spine names (118 spine concepts with variant ids)

- spine: **relative entropy / KL divergence** (information-theory) → canonical id `relative-entropy-kl-divergence`
  - `kl-divergence` — 42 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory, llm — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: asymmetric, nonnegative measure of proximity between densities; VI minimizes KL(q‖p), here with q first
  - `relative-entropy` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[polyanskiy-2025-information/ch15-universal]]: used with the meanings of the canon list
  - `relative-entropy-kl-divergence` — 42 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: $D(p\|q)$, non-negative, zero iff $p=q$, not a metric
- spine: **mutual information I(X;Y)** (information-theory) → canonical id `mutual-information-ixy`
  - `mutual-information` — 45 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory, political-science — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch02-entropy]]: $D(p(x,y)\|p(x)p(y))=H(X)-H(X|Y)$; symmetric; $\ge0$, $=0$ iff independent
  - `mutual-information-ixy` — 13 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: reduction in uncertainty, $H(X)-H(X|Y)$; symmetric, non-negative
- spine: **bounded rationality / heuristics** (economics-finance) → canonical id `bounded-rationality-heuristics`
  - `bounded-rationality` — 10 digest(s); control-theory, decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: players may follow rules of thumb or habits, not maximize (cites Simon 1955)
  - `bounded-rationality-heuristics` — 21 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[arthur-1994-elfarol/main]]: rationality is limited by human logical capacity and by ill-defined interactive problems; Arthur proposes induction as what to put in its…
  - `heuristic` — 21 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/appa-judgment]]: a mental rule that simplifies probability judgment or value prediction; usually useful, sometimes severely wrong
- spine: **entropy H(X)** (information-theory) → canonical id `entropy-hx`
  - `entropy` — 29 digest(s); bayesian, communication-theory, complex-systems, control-theory, information-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch09-incessant]]: Shannon's $H=-\sum p\log p$; Ashby uses the word only in this sense
  - `entropy-h` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[pierce-1980-introduction/ch07-efficient]]: minimum average binary digits per symbol (or per second) needed to send a source's messages
  - `entropy-h-x` — 5 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[gibson-2019-efficiency/main]]: average surprisal of a random variable, as uncertainty about its value
  - `entropy-hx` — 15 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: $-\sum p(x)\log_2 p(x)$; average bits needed to describe $X$
- spine: **Marginal likelihood (evidence)** (bayesian) → canonical id `marginal-likelihood-evidence`
  - `marginal-likelihood` — 28 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[bayes-1763-essay/main]]: $AiB/CA$, the probability of the observed counts before $W$ is thrown
  - `marginal-likelihood-evidence` — 20 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: $p(\mathbf x)=\int p(\mathbf z,\mathbf x)d\mathbf z$; its intractability is why inference is hard; ELBO lower-bounds its log
- spine: **heuristics / cues** (political-science) → canonical id `heuristics-cues`
  - `heuristic` — 21 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/appa-judgment]]: a mental rule that simplifies probability judgment or value prediction; usually useful, sometimes severely wrong
  - `heuristics-cues` — 25 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: shortcuts such as trusted individuals, stereotypes or experience, said to let uninformed voters choose well. The authors are skeptical
- spine: **Hierarchical (multilevel) model** (bayesian) → canonical id `hierarchical-multilevel-model`
  - `hierarchical-model` — 30 digest(s); bayesian, political-science — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p2]]: ARD and LDA both place priors on parameters of lower-level distributions
  - `hierarchical-multilevel-model` — 12 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch07-evaluating]]: used for the eight schools; replication can be for existing or new groups
- spine: **typical set / AEP** (information-theory) → canonical id `typical-set-aep`
  - `typical-set` — 11 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[betancourt-2017-hmc/main-p1]]: region between mode and tails, where density × volume is significant; invariant under reparameterization
  - `typical-set-aep` — 30 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: about $2^{nH}$ sequences, each of probability about $2^{-nH}$
- spine: **Bellman equation / value function** (control-theory) → canonical id `bellman-equation-value-function`
  - `bellman-equation` — 5 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch10-x]]: here the functional equation $f=\max_G\min_{G'}\iint[R+h\,f(T,T')]$
  - `bellman-equation-value-function` — 29 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch01-i-p1]]: $f_N(x)=\max_y[g(y)+h(x-y)+f_{N-1}(ay+b(x-y))]$, with $f$ the maximal return
  - `bellman-equations` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch19-reinforcement]]: self-consistency relations linking values at $t$ and $t+1$
  - `value-function` — 2 digest(s); control-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch04-value]]: S-shaped curve over gains and losses from a reference point
- spine: **feedback (positive/negative)** (complex-systems) → canonical id `feedback-positive-negative`
  - `feedback` — 31 digest(s); communication-theory, complex-systems, control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch04-machine]]: circularity of action: each of two parts affects the other (p. 53); Ashby says the exact definition is unimportant
  - `feedback-positive-negative` — 3 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[mitchell-2009-guidedtour/ch19-past]]: a central cybernetic concept; also Mitchell's two-way cycle between specific systems and common principles
- spine: **mental accounting, endowment effect, loss aversion** (economics-finance) → canonical id `mental-accounting-endowment-effect-loss-aversion`
  - `endowment-effect` — 6 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/appb-choices]]: reluctance to give up owned assets; buying price below selling price
  - `loss-aversion` — 10 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[kahneman-1979-prospect/main]]: losses loom larger than gains: $v(x)<-v(-x)$, and $v'(x)<v'(-x)$ if the derivative exists
  - `mental-accounting` — 7 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/appb-choices]]: evaluating options through accounts of advantages and disadvantages, minimal, topical or comprehensive
  - `mental-accounting-endowment-effect-loss-aversion` — 9 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch04-value]]: loss aversion: losses hurt about twice as much as equal gains; explains the endowment effect
- spine: **Principle of indifference / maximum entropy** (bayesian) → canonical id `principle-of-indifference-maximum-entropy`
  - `maximum-entropy` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch02-probability-p2]]: closest distribution to prior $q$ in KL under feature-expectation constraints; with a uniform prior this is maximizing entropy
  - `principle-of-indifference` — 10 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[griffiths-2006-optimal/main]]: related only loosely: the fixed uninformative prior $1/t_{total}$ is an alternative the data are inconsistent with
  - `principle-of-indifference-maximum-entropy` — 19 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[bayes-1763-essay/main]]: the scholium argues from having no reason to expect one count rather than another; these pages do not call it a principle
- spine: **Frequentist vs Bayesian inference** (bayesian) → canonical id `frequentist-vs-bayesian-inference`
  - `bayesian-inference` — 4 digest(s); control-theory, decision-game-theory, linear-algebra, llm — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch08-when]]: compute the full posterior p(θ | X) (8.22) and predict by (8.23)
  - `frequentist-vs-bayesian-inference` — 25 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[efron-1986-bayesian/main]]: the contrast the whole paper is about; per Efron, the parting of the ways is $\theta$ averages (expectations with the state of nature $\t…
- spine: **Partial pooling / shrinkage** (bayesian) → canonical id `partial-pooling-shrinkage`
  - `partial-pooling` — 6 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/appc-computation]]: a half-Cauchy prior on τ gives a lower posterior mean and median of τ and greater shrinkage of θ_j than the near-uniform prior
  - `partial-pooling-shrinkage` — 23 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[efron-1986-bayesian/main]]: James–Stein estimation, cited as an idea that fails the objectivity test and, via its Bayesian explanation, as a hopeful prototype
- spine: **Confounding vs selection bias** (bayesian) → canonical id `confounding-vs-selection-bias`
  - `confounding` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch04-confounding]]: anything making P(Y | X) differ from P(Y | do(X)); originally "mixing" of true and spurious effects
  - `confounding-vs-selection-bias` — 26 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch06-graphical]]: bias from common causes vs from conditioning on common effects; both are bias under the null
  - `selection-bias` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch08-selection]]: association from conditioning on a common effect of two variables, one the treatment or its cause, the other the outcome or its cause
- spine: **Variational inference & ELBO** (bayesian) → canonical id `variational-inference-elbo`
  - `elbo` — 2 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch17-variational]]: lower bound on log Pr(x|ϕ) from Jensen's inequality; equals log-likelihood minus KL to the posterior
  - `variational-inference-elbo` — 26 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: approximate the posterior by optimizing a member of a family $\mathcal Q$ to minimize KL(q‖p(z|x)), equivalently maximize the ELBO
- spine: **Markov chain Monte Carlo (MCMC)** (bayesian) → canonical id `markov-chain-monte-carlo-mcmc`
  - `markov-chain-monte-carlo` — 12 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[betancourt-2017-hmc/main-p1]]: a chain whose transition preserves the target; its history estimates expectations
  - `markov-chain-monte-carlo-mcmc` — 10 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: the baseline: sample an ergodic chain with the posterior as stationary law; asymptotically exact but heavier
  - `mcmc` — 4 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch11-basics]]: Markov chain Monte Carlo: sequential draws whose distribution converges to p(θ|y); the Markov property helps prove this but is not the key
- spine: **orthonormal basis / orthogonal matrix** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `orthonormal-basis-orthogonal-matrix`
  - `orthogonal-matrix` — 3 digest(s); linear-algebra, llm — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch04-matrix-p2]]: $U,V$ in the SVD; $A^\top=A^{-1}$
  - `orthonormal-basis` — 4 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch06-inner-p2]]: orthonormal list that is a basis; gives coordinates $\langle v,e_k\rangle$
  - `orthonormal-basis-orthogonal-matrix` — 18 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch07-operators-p2]]: the unitary matrix/operator (conjugate-transpose version): $S^*S=SS^*=I$, columns and rows orthonormal
- spine: **principal–agent problem / delegation** (political-science) → canonical id `principal-agent-problem-delegation`
  - `delegation` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[schelling-1960-conflict/ch05-enforcement-p2]]: handing interest or decision to an agent with different incentives or firmer resolve
  - `principal-agent-problem` — 14 digest(s); economics-finance, llm, political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/app-retrospective]]: voter as principal using a threshold rule on an incumbent agent whose effort he cannot observe
  - `principal-agent-problem-delegation` — 10 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch12-parliamentary-p3]]: difficulties when a principal delegates to an agent with possibly different goals who cannot be perfectly monitored
- spine: **Subjective (personal) probability** (bayesian) → canonical id `subjective-personal-probability`
  - `subjective-personal-probability` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[efron-1986-bayesian/main]]: de Finetti–Savage subjective Bayes, which has the legitimate claim to philosophic coherency but, per Efron, has failed to make much of a …
  - `subjective-probability` — 23 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch01-probability]]: here, probability as a yardstick of uncertainty. The authors argue that all statistical methods using probability rely on idealisations, …
- spine: **Cross-validation / LOO / WAIC** (bayesian) → canonical id `cross-validation-loo-waic`
  - `cross-validation` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch13-least]]: k-fold rotation; checks a choice of basis functions via RMS cross-validation error (13.4)
  - `cross-validation-loo-waic` — 17 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[efron-1986-bayesian/main]]: the nearest spine concept; Efron's bootstrap analysis for the tree is described as much like a cross-validation, correcting the apparent …
  - `waic` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch06-overfitting-p2]]: $-2(\text{lppd}-p_{\text{WAIC}})$; pointwise, no Gaussian assumption
- spine: **delay (dead time)** (control-theory) → canonical id `delay-dead-time`
  - `delay` — 6 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch08-transmission]]: here a device repeating its input one step later (p. 147–148). Also the slow transfer of variety when part of the sender acts only throug…
  - `delay-dead-time` — 18 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch12-error]]: R cannot complete its action before T's outcome starts
- spine: **dominant / dominated strategy** (decision-game-theory) → canonical id `dominant-dominated-strategy`
  - `dominant-dominated-strategy` — 22 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: defection is best whatever the other does, so two egoists in a one-shot game both defect
  - `dominated-strategy` — 2 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch04-mixed-p1]]: strict domination may be by a mixed strategy; strictly dominated actions never used in equilibrium, weakly dominated ones may be
- spine: **null space (kernel)** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `null-space-kernel`
  - `null-space` — 9 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch05-eigenvalues-p1]]: the kernel; here eigenvectors for $\lambda$ are the nonzero elements of $\operatorname{null}(T-\lambda I)$
  - `null-space-kernel` — 15 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p1]]: $\{v: Tv=0\}$, a subspace of the domain
- spine: **positive (semi)definite matrix** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `positive-semidefinite-matrix`
  - `positive-definite-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch09-linear]]: here S(x) positive definite everywhere makes F convex; the model bowl has S symmetric positive definite
  - `positive-semidefinite-matrix` — 23 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch07-operators-p2]]: Axler's "positive operator": self-adjoint with $\langle Tv,v\rangle\ge0$; "positive definite" (matrix) needs $>0$ for $x\neq0$
- spine: **Convergence diagnostics (R-hat, ESS, divergences)** (bayesian) → canonical id `convergence-diagnostics-r-hat-ess-divergences`
  - `convergence-diagnostics` — 20 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[betancourt-2017-hmc/main-p1]]: here: split $\hat R$ as the empirical diagnostic, with geometric ergodicity as the formal sufficient condition (p. 15); ESS measures esti…
  - `convergence-diagnostics-r-hat-ess-divergences` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2020-workflow/main-p1]]: $\widehat R<1.01$, $R^*$, effective sample size, absence of divergent transitions; they cannot detect a model coded differently than inte…
  - `convergence-diagnostics-rhat-ess-divergences` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch08-markov]]: here `n_eff` (effective sample count) and Rhat (Gelman–Rubin). Rhat may be seen "perhaps as a signal of danger, but never of safety"
- spine: **strategic (normal-form) game** (decision-game-theory) → canonical id `strategic-normal-form-game`
  - `normal-form` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[strogatz-2024-nonlinear/ch03-bifurcations-p1]]: prototypical equation that the dynamics near a bifurcation typically look like, e.g. $\dot x=r-x^2$
  - `strategic-game` — 4 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch09-cooperation]]: a situation where each person's payoff depends on everyone's choices; used for commuters, typists, drivers
  - `strategic-normal-form-game` — 18 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: the 2×2 payoff matrix of Figure 1
- spine: **validity / soundness** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `validity-soundness`
  - `validity` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch18-constructing]]: the chapter treats finding a proof as the way to show that an argument is valid
  - `validity-soundness` — 21 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch02-logic]]: here only deductive validity is described: true premises force a true conclusion
- spine: **cross-entropy / log-loss** (llm) → canonical id `cross-entropy-log-loss`
  - `cross-entropy` — 4 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch06-overfitting-p1]]: $H(p,q)=-\sum p_i\log q_i$; $D_{KL}=H(p,q)-H(p)$
  - `cross-entropy-log-loss` — 18 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[bahdanau-2015-attention/main]]: training maximises the conditional log-probability of correct translations (NLL in Table 2)
- spine: **paradigm / normal science** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `paradigm-normal-science`
  - `normal-science` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[kuhn-1962-structure/ch01-introduction]]: research firmly based on received commitments, taught in education; it tries to fit nature into "conceptual boxes"
  - `paradigm` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, control-theory — e.g. [[kuhn-1962-structure/ch10-revolutions]]: shared framework that fixes what counts as a datum, instrument and relevant concept; here also what is seen (pp. 112, 122). *(canon: "par…
  - `paradigm-normal-science` — 18 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch05-kuhn]]: a whole way of doing science, including exemplars, which guides the choice of puzzles; normal science is the work done within it. Broad s…
- spine: **Homophily / heterophily** (communication-theory) → canonical id `homophily-heterophily`
  - `homophily` — 2 digest(s); communication-theory, political-science — e.g. [[bail-2018-opposingviews/main]]: tendency to form network ties with similar others
  - `homophily-heterophily` — 19 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[katz-1955-personalinfluence/ch10-marketing]]: same-status pairing resembles homophily; the term is not used in this unit (canon link; not stated in this unit)
- spine: **political awareness / sophistication** (political-science) → canonical id `political-awareness-sophistication`
  - `political-awareness` — 6 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch10-it]]: measured by factual knowledge; here it raises partisan bias in perceptions and ripples
  - `political-awareness-sophistication` — 15 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch11-groups]]: in this chapter, more engaged citizens show more partisan bias, and sophistication does not guarantee better judgment
- spine: **Assignment mechanism / ignorability** (bayesian) → canonical id `assignment-mechanism-ignorability`
  - `assignment-mechanism-ignorability` — 19 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch08-modeling]]: design is ignorable when $p(\theta|x,y_{\rm obs})=p(\theta|x,y_{\rm obs},I)$; sufficient: missing at random plus distinct parameters
  - `ignorability` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch24-causal]]: potential outcomes independent of treatment assignment; delivered by randomization (p. 412) (new)
- spine: **Hamiltonian Monte Carlo (HMC/NUTS)** (bayesian) → canonical id `hamiltonian-monte-carlo-hmc-nuts`
  - `hamiltonian-monte-carlo` — 16 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[betancourt-2017-hmc/main-p2]]: MCMC that lifts $q$ to phase space, runs Hamiltonian trajectories, and projects back (p. 26). Canon id: Hamiltonian Monte Carlo (HMC/NUTS).
  - `hamiltonian-monte-carlo-hmc-nuts` — 4 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2020-workflow/main-p1]]: a generalisation of Metropolis that uses gradients to move efficiently in continuous spaces
- spine: **logical form vs. grammatical form** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `logical-form-vs-grammatical-form`
  - `logical-form` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch04-falsifiability]]: whether a hypothesis is falsifiable concerns only its logical relation to possible basic statements (p. 67), but logical form alone canno…
  - `logical-form-vs-grammatical-form` — 19 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch02-definite]]: surface subject–predicate form can hide quantificational structure, as with "nobody"
- spine: **Stereotype (Lippmann)** (communication-theory) → canonical id `stereotype-lippmann`
  - `stereotype` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch16-causes]]: group statement accepted, at least tentatively, as fact about every member; used neutrally by Kahneman
  - `stereotype-lippmann` — 18 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[carey-1989-cultural/ch03-reconceiving]]: what news spreads where no good machinery of record exists
- spine: **self-information / surprisal** (information-theory) → canonical id `self-information-surprisal`
  - `self-information` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch20-example]]: here only the objective $\log 1/P(\{x\})$ appears
  - `self-information-surprisal` — 16 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch02-entropy]]: here "self-information" means $I(X;X)=H(X)$, the entropy of a variable; the text does not use the per-outcome $-\log p(x)$ sense
  - `surprisal` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[frank-2012-rsa/main]]: $-\log p(x)$, how much information is gained by observing $x$; the measure of informativeness
- spine: **agenda control / agenda setter** (political-science) → canonical id `agenda-control-agenda-setter`
  - `agenda-control` — 6 digest(s); decision-game-theory, political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch03-tumbling]]: wealthy interests can shape which policies reach a majority, "through agenda-setting and framing"
  - `agenda-control-agenda-setter` — 11 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: institutions and those shaping the menu of alternatives determine outcomes when tastes yield no equilibrium
  - `agenda-setter` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch05-spatial-p2]]: one who controls the order and menu of votes. In McKelvey's world can steer to any point
- spine: **exponential family / Gibbs distribution** (information-theory) → canonical id `exponential-family-gibbs-distribution`
  - `exponential-family` — 8 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch12-information-p2]]: P*(a) ∝ Q(a)e^{λa²} and P_λ ∝ P₁^λP₂^{1−λ} are exponential tilts
  - `exponential-family-gibbs-distribution` — 10 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-1957-information/main]]: max-entropy solution $p_i=\exp\{-[\lambda_0+\sum\lambda_kf_k(x_i)]\}$
- spine: **Fano's inequality** (information-theory) → canonical id `fanos-inequality`
  - `fano-inequality` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[polyanskiy-2025-information/ch19-error]]: gives the same bound in one line
  - `fanos-inequality` — 16 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch02-entropy]]: $H(P_e)+P_e\log(|\mathcal X|-1)\ge H(X|Y)$
- spine: **Boltzmann (softmax) rationality** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `boltzmann-softmax-rationality`
  - `boltzmann-softmax-rationality` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[frank-2012-rsa/main]]: (canon link; the paper says "Luce choice rule", not this name) speaker choice ∝ $e^{\alpha U}$ (S1). $\alpha$ is the decision-noise param…
  - `softmax` — 16 digest(s); information-theory, llm — e.g. [[bahdanau-2015-attention/main]]: turns alignment scores $e_{ij}$ into weights $\alpha_{ij}$ that sum to 1 over source positions
- spine: **negative (balancing) feedback** (control-theory) → canonical id `negative-balancing-feedback`
  - `negative-balancing-feedback` — 10 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch12-error]]: for a regulator that must be stable with the desired temperature as its equilibrium, the feedback around the circuit must "usually" be ne…
  - `negative-feedback` — 7 digest(s); control-theory, information-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch05-stability]]: loop in which a returning displacement diminishes the initial one; a simple stability test that is unreliable if complications are present
- spine: **Bayesian Occam's razor** (bayesian) → canonical id `bayesian-occams-razor`
  - `bayesian-occam-razor` — 2 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch24-exact]]: the Occam factor in (24.13), a volume factor from integrating out μ
  - `bayesian-occams-razor` — 14 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch22-finite]]: the "intrinsic Bayes penalty": marginal likelihood tends to fall as items are spread across more components
- spine: **zero-sum game & minimax** (decision-game-theory) → canonical id `zero-sum-game-minimax`
  - `zero-sum-game` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch10-x]]: two players with opposed payoffs, $b_{ij}=-a_{ij}$; the only case with a satisfactory general theory
  - `zero-sum-game-minimax` — 15 digest(s); decision-game-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: the Senate case is "hardly" zero-sum. Chess contrasts as a game of completely antagonistic interests, where assuming the most feared move…
- spine: **column space (range)** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `column-space-range`
  - `column-space` — 7 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch06-inner-p2]]: appear as $\operatorname{null}T$ and $\operatorname{range}T$ and their complements
  - `column-space-range` — 8 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p1]]: here the range of any linear map $T$: all outputs $Tv$, a subspace of the target (p. 61); no matrices yet
- spine: **pretraining / fine-tuning** (llm) → canonical id `pretraining-fine-tuning`
  - `fine-tuning` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch32-representation]]: adapt all network parameters (plus a new layer) on the downstream task
  - `pretraining-fine-tuning` — 14 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[brown-2020-gpt3/main-p1]]: the paradigm the paper contrasts with: weights updated on thousands to hundreds of thousands of task examples
- spine: **temporal-difference learning / reward prediction error** (control-theory) → canonical id `temporal-difference-learning-reward-prediction-error`
  - `temporal-difference-learning` — 7 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sutton-2018-rl/ch01-introduction]]: updates driven by the difference between estimates at successive times
  - `temporal-difference-learning-reward-prediction-error` — 8 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[recht-2019-tour/main]]: value-function methods derived from the value-centred view; named but not derived here

  - `cycling-intransitivity` — 13 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: the problem behind "chaos theorems": any policy can be beaten by another in a majority vote
- spine: **dot (inner) product** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `dot-inner-product`
  - `dot-inner-product` — 12 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch01-vectors]]: $a^Tb=\sum a_ib_i$, called inner or dot product here
  - `dot-product` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deerwester-1990-lsa/main]]: dot product or cosine between vectors in the factor space estimates similarity; cosine is used to rank documents
- spine: **efficient market hypothesis (weak/semi-strong/strong)** (economics-finance) → canonical id `efficient-market-hypothesis-weak-semi-strong-strong`
  - `efficient-market-hypothesis` — 4 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[grossman-1980-impossibility/main]]: author: "prices fully reflect all available information" (Fama) is, with costly information, inconsistent with competitive equilibrium
  - `efficient-market-hypothesis-weak-semi-strong-strong` — 10 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[fama-1970-efficient/main]]: prices at any time "fully reflect" an information set: past prices (weak), obviously public information (semi-strong), all information in…
- spine: **reference (set point)** (control-theory) → canonical id `reference-set-point`
  - `reference` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch02-feedback]]: the signal $r$ giving the desired output
  - `reference-set-point` — 13 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch12-error]]: Ashby's η is the given set of acceptable states of E, not a single value (p. 219). The match is loose.
- spine: **strategic (sophisticated) voting** (political-science) → canonical id `strategic-sophisticated-voting`
  - `strategic-sophisticated-voting` — 9 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[arrow-1951-socialchoice/ch01-i]]: misrepresenting tastes by action when profitable; here the "game aspect" excluded
  - `strategic-voting` — 5 digest(s); decision-game-theory, political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p1]]: here, strategic means voting for one's most preferred *viable* candidate (p. 545). Canon id: [[strategic-sophisticated-voting]].

  - `theory-of-mind` — 7 digest(s); economics-finance, llm, theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: ascribing beliefs, desires and intentions to explain, predict and justify behaviour
  - `theory-of-mind-mentalizing-mindreading` — 7 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2011-mindreaders/coda]]: here "mindreading": ascribing mental states, studied by Apperly mainly for epistemic states
- spine: **heavy tail / power law** (complex-systems) → canonical id `heavy-tail-power-law`
  - `heavy-tail-power-law` — 12 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[bak-1987-soc/main]]: $D(s)\sim s^{-\tau}$ and $D(t)\sim t^{-\alpha}$ at criticality
  - `power-law` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[kaplan-2020-scaling/main]]: relation $y\propto x^{-\alpha}$, a straight line on log-log axes; fitted separately for N, D, C

  - `selective-exposure-media-choice` — 2 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[bail-2018-opposingviews/main]]: people's choice of information that fits their views; the paper tries to *disrupt* it experimentally
- spine: **Arrow's impossibility theorem** (political-science) → canonical id `arrows-impossibility-theorem`
  - `arrow-impossibility-theorem` — 5 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch11-groups]]: cited as illuminating the folk theory's internal contradictions
  - `arrows-impossibility-theorem` — 7 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: any process meeting certain reasonable conditions faces similar difficulties as majority rule

  - `back-door-front-door-criterion` — 11 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch19-time]]: used in the generalized backdoor criterion and SWIG checks
  - `front-door-criterion` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch07-confounding]]: identification via a fully mediating $M$, using $\sum_m\Pr[M=m|A=a]\sum_{a'}\Pr[Y=1|M=m,A=a']\Pr[A=a']$
- spine: **conditions for persuasion (Lupia–McCubbins)** (political-science) → canonical id `conditions-for-persuasion-lupia-mccubbins`
  - `conditions-for-persuasion` — 3 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch04-people]]: persuasion requires that the listener perceive the speaker as knowledgeable and trustworthy; the basis for trust can come from external f…
  - `conditions-for-persuasion-lupia-mccubbins` — 9 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/app-ch03]]: trusted speaker, perceived knowledge, and a signal showing the prior is insufficient: individually necessary, collectively sufficient
- spine: **Cox's theorem** (bayesian) → canonical id `coxs-theorem`
  - `cox-theorem` — 9 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/appa-other]]: consistency forces the sum and product rules once plausibilities are real numbers
  - `coxs-theorem` — 3 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch15-paradoxes]]: the finite-set derivation of the rules; Jaynes accepts well-defined limits
- spine: **sense (Sinn) vs. reference (Bedeutung)** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `sense-sinn-vs-reference-bedeutung`
  - `reference` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch02-feedback]]: the signal $r$ giving the desired output
  - `sense-sinn-vs-reference-bedeutung` — 10 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[kripke-1980-naming/ch01-lecture-p1]]: Frege: the description gives the name's sense; Kripke uses "referent" for the object satisfying it
  - `sense-vs-reference` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch05-traditional]]: only touched on: Frege's abstract "thoughts" and "senses" are mentioned in passing, and the sense/reference contrast itself is not discus…
- spine: **singular value decomposition (SVD)** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `singular-value-decomposition-svd`
  - `singular-value-decomposition` — 7 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch07-operators-p3]]: $Tv=\sum s_k\langle v,e_k\rangle f_k$ with orthonormal lists in $V$ and $W$ (7.70); gives a diagonal matrix in suitable orthonormal bases
  - `singular-value-decomposition-svd` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch04-matrix-p2]]: $A=U\Sigma V^\top$ for any real $m\times n$ matrix, with $U,V$ orthogonal and $\Sigma$ diagonal, non-negative
- spine: **strange attractor / fractal** (complex-systems) → canonical id `strange-attractor-fractal`

  - `strange-attractor-fractal` — 11 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[bak-1987-soc/main]]: fractal structures proposed as minimally stable states that stop at criticality

  - `attractor-basin-of-attraction` — 10 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[bak-1987-soc/main]]: the critical state is an attractor reached from far from equilibrium
  - `basin-of-attraction` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch42-hopfield]]: set of initial states flowing to a given attractor
- spine: **mutual vs. common knowledge** (decision-game-theory) → canonical id `mutual-vs-common-knowledge`
  - `common-knowledge` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/app-ch02]]: the authors assume all elements of every model are common knowledge, unless stated otherwise; the term is not defined on these pages
  - `mutual-vs-common-knowledge` — 10 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch04-beautiful]]: the "obvious that it is obvious that…" regress

  - `observer` — 4 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch09-transfer-p1]]: state estimator (9.33), combined into a controller transfer function
  - `observer-state-estimator` — 7 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch08-output]]: model copy plus output-error correction $L(y-C\hat x)$ so that $\hat x\to x$

  - `phase-space` — 3 digest(s); bayesian, complex-systems, control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch03-determinate]]: the plane or space where states are points and the transformation is drawn as arrows
  - `state-space-phase-space` — 8 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[lorenz-1963-nonperiodic/main]]: $M$-dimensional space whose points are states and whose curves are trajectories

  - `structural-causal-model-scm` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2016-primer/ch02-graphical]]: functions plus exogenous errors; graph-implied independencies hold for any functions if errors are independent

  - `common-pool-resource` — 4 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch16-consequences-p2]]: actors use a shared resource while paying only a share of its cost, so they over-consume it
  - `common-pool-resource-cpr` — 6 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[ostrom-1990-commons/ch02-institutional]]: resource system large enough that excluding beneficiaries is costly but not impossible; units are subtractable
- spine: **confirmation holism (Quine–Duhem thesis)** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `confirmation-holism-quine-duhem-thesis`
  - `confirmation-holism` — 3 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch03-induction]]: the relevance of an observation depends on other assumptions; used for Good's reply
  - `confirmation-holism-quine-duhem-thesis` — 6 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch02-logic]]: only networks of claims, not single claims, make testable predictions
  - `quine-duhem-thesis` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-philosophy/main]]: the claim (invoked by Kuhn) that one is always free to blame auxiliary hypotheses for failed predictions
- spine: **perspective-taking (level 1 / level 2)** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `perspective-taking-level-1-level-2`
  - `perspective-taking-level-1` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[ullman-2023-trivial-alterations/main]]: knowing what another can perceive; perceptual access is varied in variants 1A and 2A
  - `perspective-taking-level-1-level-2` — 9 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: level-2 needs sensitivity to modes of presentation
- spine: **positive (reinforcing) feedback** (control-theory) → canonical id `positive-reinforcing-feedback`
  - `positive-feedback` — 4 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch05-stability]]: each variable raises the other; Ashby shows this can still be stable
  - `positive-reinforcing-feedback` — 6 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch01-introduction]]: an increase leads to further increase; destabilizing, usually limited by saturation

  - `agenda-setting` — 4 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[lazer-2018-fakenews/main]]: media typically cannot change attitudes but are far more successful at changing what we think about
  - `priming` — 3 digest(s); communication-theory, decision-game-theory — e.g. [[entman-1993-framing/main]]: as in Sniderman et al. (quoted): framing primes values differentially, establishing salience of one or another
  - `priming-agenda-setting` — 3 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[chong-2007-framing/main]]: changes in the standards used to evaluate leaders after media emphasis; authors argue it shares processes with framing
- spine: **propositional attitude** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `propositional-attitude`
  - `propositional-attitude` — 9 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: a belief is an attitude to a content; adults' content is propositional
  - `propositional-attitudes` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[wittgenstein-1922-tractatus/ch00-introduction]]: believing, doubting, desiring, etc., covered by the problem of the logical form of belief; Russell analyses belief as a relation between …
- spine: **controllability (reachability)** (control-theory) → canonical id `controllability-reachability`
  - `controllability` — 5 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch03-system-p1]]: here called *reachability*: can possible states be reached by choosing $u$; only named, treated in Chs. 7 and 8
  - `controllability-reachability` — 4 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch07-state]]: any state reachable from any state in finite time; tested by rank of $W_r$ (Def. 7.1, Thm 7.1; pp. 7-2, 7-4). Author prefers "reachabilit…
- spine: **linear map / transformation** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `linear-map-transformation`
  - `linear-map` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch04-polynomials]]: appears only in the exercises (Ex. 10, 14)
  - `linear-map-transformation` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p1]]: function $T:V\to W$ with additivity and homogeneity; Axler writes $Tv$ and calls $\mathcal L(V,W)$ the set of them
  - `transformation` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch02-change]]: a set of transitions on a set of operands, defined by what each becomes, not why

- spine: **modularity (ToMM)** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `modularity-tomm`
  - `modularity` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[rosvall-2008-maps/main-p1]]: $Q$ of Eq. 2: in-module link weight minus its expected value
  - `modularity-tomm` — 8 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p1]]: belief ascription by modules, fast, structures fixed in infancy (Leslie's view as reported)

  - `rank-nullity` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch05-eigenvalues-p1]]: used as the fundamental theorem of linear maps (3.21) in 5.34
  - `rank-nullity-fundamental-theorem-of-linear-maps` — 4 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p1]]: $\dim V=\dim\operatorname{null}T+\dim\operatorname{range}T$ for finite-dimensional $V$

  - `reciprocity` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: helping a colleague and being repaid in kind; Senate norm
  - `reciprocity-direct-indirect-network` — 6 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch04-live]]: answering restraint with restraint and defection with retaliation
- spine: **saddle-node (fold) bifurcation** (complex-systems) → canonical id `saddle-node-fold-bifurcation`
  - `saddle-node-bifurcation` — 4 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[castellano-2009-socialdynamics/main-p3]]: in the $\sigma$-modified model, two stable fixed points coexist above a critical density
  - `saddle-node-fold-bifurcation` — 5 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[scheffer-2009-earlywarning/main]]: equilibrium curve folded backwards; the unstable middle branch marks the basin border; dominant eigenvalue becomes zero at the fold point…

  - `criticality-phase-transition` — 5 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[anderson-1972-moreisdifferent/main]]: in the $N\to\infty$ limit, matter undergoes sharp, singular transitions to less symmetric states (p. 395) (canon link; not stated in this…
  - `phase-transition` — 3 digest(s); information-theory, llm — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch31-ising]]: a 'collective phenomenon'; true phase transitions only for infinite $N$, hinted at by relatively bigger fluctuations at intermediate temp…

  - `fiedler-vector` — 3 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch24-nodal]]: $\psi_2$ and its sign split, as in the drawings
  - `fiedler-vector-algebraic-connectivity` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch01-introduction]]: $\lambda_2$ of the Laplacian, proposed by Fiedler as a measure of connectivity; zero iff the graph is disconnected

  - `doubling-rate` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch15-information]]: $W(\mathbf b,F)=E\log\mathbf b^t\mathbf X$, the almost-sure exponential growth rate of reinvested wealth
  - `kelly-criterion` — 2 digest(s); economics-finance, information-theory — e.g. [[kelly-1956-information/main]]: bet fractions chosen to maximize the exponential growth rate $G$, equivalently the expected log of capital
  - `kelly-criterion-doubling-rate` — 5 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: $W$ in (1.7), the maximal asymptotic growth exponent of wealth

  - `tokenization` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch05-bag]]: splitting a document into tokens; types form the vocabulary
  - `tokenization-bpe` — 7 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[brown-2020-gpt3/main-p1]]: byte-level BPE inherited from GPT-2, about 0.7 words per token; the authors suggest it may hurt En→Ro and complicates character-level tasks

  - `independence-of-irrelevant-alternatives` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory, political-science — e.g. [[arrow-1951-socialchoice/ch03-iii]]: choice from $S$ depends only on individual orderings over $S$ (Condition 3)
  - `independence-of-irrelevant-alternatives-iia` — 4 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[arrow-1951-socialchoice/ch04-iv]]: Arrow's Condition 3 as used here: x–y choice should not depend on orderings of alternatives outside $[x,y]$ (p. 40) (canon link; name not…
- spine: **Markov (stochastic) matrix** (linear-algebra) → canonical id `markov-stochastic-matrix`
  - `markov-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch31-psrgs]]: $W=M/d$, the ordinary random-walk transition matrix
  - `markov-stochastic-matrix` — 6 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[bryan-2006-eigenvector/main]]: column-stochastic: entries $\ge0$, columns sum to 1; always has eigenvalue 1
- spine: **reality bias / curse of knowledge** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `reality-bias-curse-of-knowledge`
  - `reality-bias` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[wimmer-1983-beliefs-about-beliefs/main]]: the egocentric reading of "wrong" or "true" against one's own knowledge, instead of the protagonist's belief
  - `reality-bias-curse-of-knowledge` — 6 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2009-two-systems/main-p2]]: egocentric bias in adults' belief reasoning
- spine: **Receive–Accept–Sample (RAS) model** (political-science) → canonical id `receive-accept-sample-ras-model`
  - `receive-accept-sample-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch09-two]]: opinion as a response to considerations received, accepted and sampled. Here extended to two competing messages
  - `receive-accept-sample-ras-model` — 6 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch04-coming]]: axioms A1–A4: reception, resistance, accessibility and response; treated as an information-processing model

  - `supervised-fine-tuning-sft` — 3 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[ouyang-2022-instructgpt/main-p1]]: fine-tuning GPT-3 on labeler demonstrations; here run 16 epochs despite validation-loss overfitting after 1

  - `knowledge` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch01-knowledge]]: the ability to predict the consequences of one's actions; the authors' requirement for reasoned choice, which is not the same as information
  - `knowledge-vs-ignorance-attribution` — 5 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[apperly-2011-mindreaders/ch02-evidence]]: judging that someone who has not seen does not know

  - `rational-agent-assumption` — 3 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch02-true]]: start from ideal rationality (believe implications, no contradictions) and revise downward; attribute what the system *ought* to have
  - `rational-agent-assumption-principle-of-efficiency` — 3 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch04-making]]: here the assumption that agents are rational, interpreted as "cognitive approval", not deductive closure or perfect consistency
- spine: **revolutionary threshold / cascade** (political-science) → canonical id `revolutionary-threshold-cascade`
  - `cascade` — 3 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[bak-1987-soc/main]]: the "domino" effect that spreads a perturbation through minimally stable sites
  - `revolutionary-threshold-cascade` — 3 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch08-democratic-p1]]: protest size at which a person joins; a chain of joiners is a cascade
- spine: **scientific realism vs. constructive empiricism** (analytic-philosophy) → canonical id `scientific-realism-vs-constructive-empiricism`
  - `constructive-empiricism` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch12-scientific]]: van Fraassen's view that science should aim at no more than empirical adequacy
  - `scientific-realism` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch02-logic]]: the author's view that science often describes hidden structures behind observable phenomena
  - `scientific-realism-vs-constructive-empiricism` — 3 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch13-explanation]]: van Fraassen's "pragmatic" view puts explanation outside science, against the author's internal view
- spine: **social choice function / social welfare function** (political-science) → canonical id `social-choice-function-social-welfare-function`
  - `social-choice-function-social-welfare-function` — 5 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[arrow-1951-socialchoice/ch01-i]]: procedure from individual tastes to a social choice pattern, here discussed as welfare maximization
  - `social-welfare-function` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[arrow-1951-socialchoice/ch03-iii]]: rule mapping each profile of individual orderings to a social ordering (Def. 4)

  - `bias-variance-tradeoff` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch11-model]]: protection against misspecification bias costs wider confidence intervals

  - `framing` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch02-endowment]]: equivalent descriptions (discount vs surcharge) can change behaviour
  - `framing-entman` — 2 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[entman-1993-framing/main]]: selecting some aspects of perceived reality and making them more salient to promote a problem definition, causal interpretation, moral ev…

  - `mdl` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch11-maximum]]: choosing $p$ by a two-stage description length

  - `minimum-description-length-mdl` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch28-model]]: prefer the model giving the shortest total message; $L=-\log_2P$, so MDL maps onto Bayesian comparison
- spine: **recursive mindreading / orders of intentionality** (theory-of-mind) → canonical id `recursive-mindreading-orders-of-intentionality`
  - `recursive-mindreading` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[premack-1978-chimpanzee/main]]: embedded states (one person's state about another's); the authors say only about four steps make humans uncomfortable
  - `recursive-mindreading-orders-of-intentionality` — 2 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch07-intentional]]: first-order systems lack attitudes about attitudes; higher orders embed them; Dennett suspects most people can keep track of only about f…
- spine: **common knowledge vs public information** (economics-finance) → canonical id `common-knowledge-vs-public-information`
  - `common-knowledge` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/app-ch02]]: the authors assume all elements of every model are common knowledge, unless stated otherwise; the term is not defined on these pages
  - `common-knowledge-vs-public-information` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[morris-2002-public/main]]: public signal $y$ is common knowledge; it is a focal point for beliefs
- spine: **Encoding/decoding** (communication-theory) → canonical id `encoding-decoding`
  - `encoding` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[pierce-1980-introduction/ch04-encoding]]: accurate representation of one signal or message by another
  - `encoding-decoding` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[hall-1973-encoding/main]]: a programme is encoded as a meaningful discourse and then decoded; meanings I and II may not be the same
- spine: **Kelly criterion / log-optimal growth** (economics-finance) → canonical id `kelly-criterion-log-optimal-growth`
  - `kelly-criterion` — 2 digest(s); economics-finance, information-theory — e.g. [[kelly-1956-information/main]]: bet fractions chosen to maximize the exponential growth rate $G$, equivalently the expected log of capital

  - `rational-analysis` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[griffiths-2006-optimal/main]]: judging cognition against the optimal solution given world statistics (p. 767); the paper does not use this label for its own approach, a…

  - `ambiguity` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch07-ambiguity]]: a sentence has more than one meaning
  - `risk-vs-ambiguity` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-1979-prospect/main]]: weights may also be affected by vagueness; the work of Ellsberg and Fellner implies vagueness reduces weights, per the authors

  - `uniform-information-density` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[gibson-2019-efficiency/main]]: information per unit time should be roughly constant
  - `uniform-information-density-efficient-communication` — 0 digests (canon spine only)

  - `landauer-principle` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[stone-2022-information/ch10-thermodynamic]]: acquiring or erasing one bit costs at least $0.693kT$ joules
  - `landauers-principle` — 0 digests (canon spine only)

  - `normalized-maximum-likelihood` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[polyanskiy-2025-information/ch15-universal]]: Shtarkov distribution $\propto\sup_\theta P(x^n|\theta)$; minimizes worst-case pointwise redundancy
  - `normalized-maximum-likelihood-nml` — 0 digests (canon spine only)

  - `sparse-autoencoder` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[elhage-2022-superposition/main-p2]]: dictionary learning on activations to find overcomplete feature directions
