## 3. Edit distance ≤ 2 (92 groups from 115 pairs)

- group: system-1 ~ system-2 ~ system-k ~ system-s4 ~ system-s5 ~ system-t
  - `system-1` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch01-characters]]: automatic, fast, effortless, no sense of voluntary control; source of impressions, intuitions and feelings
  - `system-2` — 2 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch01-characters]]: allocates attention to effortful activity; often associated with agency, choice and concentration; in charge of self-control; usually end…
  - `system-k` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch43-natural]]: TFL rules plus strict subproofs, □I and □E
  - `system-s4` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch43-natural]]: T + R4 (import □A into a strict subproof)
  - `system-s5` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch43-natural]]: S4 + R5 (import ¬□A into a strict subproof)
  - `system-t` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch43-natural]]: K + RT (from □A infer A)
- group: entropy-h ~ entropy-h-x ~ entropy-hx
  - `entropy-h` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[pierce-1980-introduction/ch07-efficient]]: minimum average binary digits per symbol (or per second) needed to send a source's messages
  - `entropy-h-x` — 5 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[gibson-2019-efficiency/main]]: average surprisal of a random variable, as uncertainty about its value
  - `entropy-hx` — 15 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch01-introduction]]: $-\sum p(x)\log_2 p(x)$; average bits needed to describe $X$
- group: arrow-impossibility-theorem ~ arrow-s-impossibility-theorem ~ arrows-impossibility-theorem
  - `arrow-impossibility-theorem` — 5 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch11-groups]]: cited as illuminating the folk theory's internal contradictions
  - `arrow-s-impossibility-theorem` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch16-consequences-p1]]: here used as the "trilemma": at most two of transitivity, universal admissibility and nondictatorship
  - `arrows-impossibility-theorem` — 7 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: any process meeting certain reasonable conditions faces similar difficulties as majority rule
- group: codimension ~ dimension ~ vc-dimension
  - `codimension` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[strogatz-2024-nonlinear/ch03-bifurcations-p2]]: number of parameters to tune to reach a bifurcation; the cusp point is codimension-2
  - `dimension` — 10 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch02-finite]]: the length of any basis; well defined by 2.34
  - `vc-dimension` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch40-capacity]]: the largest number of points on which any labelling can be realized; K for threshold functions
- group: maximum-likelihood-estimate ~ maximum-likelihood-estimation ~ maximum-likelihood-estimator
  - `maximum-likelihood-estimate` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch06-elementary-p1]]: here, the likelihood maximizer $c/\phi$; ignores prior information
  - `maximum-likelihood-estimation` — 5 digest(s); complex-systems, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[clauset-2009-powerlaw/main-p1]]: choose parameters maximising the data's likelihood; here MLEs (3.1)–(3.7) for $\alpha$
  - `maximum-likelihood-estimator` — 2 digest(s); complex-systems, information-theory — e.g. [[clauset-2009-powerlaw/main-p2]]: α̂ maximizing the log-likelihood. For the continuous power law it is (B.4), equivalent to the Hill estimator
- group: admixture-model ~ mixture-code ~ mixture-model
  - `admixture-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch01-introduction]]: a model where each document is proportionally assigned to different categories
  - `mixture-code` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch05-symbol]]: metacode choosing the shortest of several codes plus a header; incomplete, hence suboptimal
  - `mixture-model` — 5 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch11-density]]: convex combination of base densities with mixture weights $\pi_k$
- group: computation ~ imputation ~ reputation
  - `computation` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[mitchell-2009-guidedtour/ch04-computation]]: the way information is processed ("processed via computation"); before the late 1940s it meant calculating by hand
  - `imputation` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch20-generative]]: filling missing data; multiple imputation samples $p(\mathbf X_m|\mathbf X_o)$
  - `reputation` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch08-social]]: others' belief about the strategy a player will use, built from observed behaviour with third parties
- group: a-cr-factorization ~ cr-factorization ~ lu-factorization
  - `a-cr-factorization` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch03-four-p3]]: independent columns C times R = rref without zero rows
  - `cr-factorization` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch01-vectors]]: A = CR with C the first r independent columns, R = [I F]
  - `lu-factorization` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/app1-ranks]]: A = LU, L with 1's, U with pivots; needs no row exchanges
- group: b-spline ~ bad-line ~ baseline
  - `b-spline` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch20-basis]]: piecewise cubic bump with compact support defined by knots, giving a sparse design matrix
  - `bad-line` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch03-truth-p2]]: a row with all premises true and conclusion false
  - `baseline` — 2 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bertsekas-2026-rlcourse/ch03-learning-p2]]: a subtracted constant or state-dependent term that reduces the range of the target
- group: covariance ~ invariance ~ invariant
  - `covariance` — 2 digest(s); control-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[markowitz-1952-portfolio/main]]: $\sigma_{ij}=\rho_{ij}\sigma_i\sigma_j$, how two returns move together
  - `invariance` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/appb-choices]]: preference order should not depend on how prospects are described
  - `invariant` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch05-stability]]: a statement that stays true while the system changes
- group: bayes-theorem ~ mays-theorem
  - `bayes-theorem` — 54 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, bayesian, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[bayes-1763-essay/main]]: here shown as Prop. 5/Prop. 9: probability of the first event given the second equals probability of both divided by probability of the s…
  - `mays-theorem` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch04-group]]: over a pair of alternatives, U, A, N, M hold if and only if the method is MMR
- group: f-divergence ~ kl-divergence
  - `f-divergence` — 12 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch02-probability-p3]]: $\int qf(p/q)$ with convex $f$, $f(1)=0$
  - `kl-divergence` — 42 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory, llm — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: asymmetric, nonnegative measure of proximity between densities; VI minimizes KL(q‖p), here with q first
- group: d-separation ~ separation
  - `d-separation` — 18 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch08-when]]: graph criterion implying conditional independence (8.34)
  - `separation` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch16-generalized]]: a linear combination of predictors predicts the outcome perfectly; the MLE can be undefined or unreasonable (−∞ in the 1964 voting example)
- group: framing-effect ~ priming-effect
  - `framing-effect` — 18 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance, political-science — e.g. [[achen-2016-realists/ch02-elusive]]: expressed preferences shift with wording or framing, e.g. "welfare" vs "assistance to the poor"
  - `priming-effect` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch04-associative]]: an idea makes related ideas easier to evoke, with or without awareness
- group: a-orthogonality ~ orthogonality
  - `a-orthogonality` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch35-conjugate]]: $p_i^TAp_j=0$ for $i\ne j$; decouples the minimization
  - `orthogonality` — 14 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch06-inner-p1]]: $\langle u,v\rangle=0$
- group: simpson-s-paradox ~ simpsons-paradox
  - `simpson-s-paradox` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch09-mediation-p2]]: mentioned only to exclude it from the tourniquet data
  - `simpsons-paradox` — 11 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch08-selection]]: margin note: result of ignoring common cause versus common effect
- group: dual-space ~ null-space
  - `dual-space` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p3]]: V′ = ℒ(V,𝐅), with dim V′ = dim V
  - `null-space` — 9 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch05-eigenvalues-p1]]: the kernel; here eigenvectors for $\lambda$ are the nonzero elements of $\operatorname{null}(T-\lambda I)$
- group: positivism ~ positivity
  - `positivism` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch02-buccaneers]]: view that science only describes thoughts and patterns of observation, so objective causation lacks scientific meaning
  - `positivity` — 9 digest(s); bayesian, political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch24-causal]]: every unit has some probability of each treatment value (p. 421) (new)
- group: em-algorithm ~ qr-algorithm
  - `em-algorithm` — 8 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p1]]: maximum-likelihood method for latent-variable models; assumes the posterior expectation is computable, unlike VI
  - `qr-algorithm` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: iterate A_{k+1} = R_kQ_k to reveal eigenvalues
- group: importance-resampling ~ importance-sampling
  - `importance-resampling` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch10-introduction]]: resample $k<S$ draws with probability proportional to weight, without replacement (p. 266) (new)
  - `importance-sampling` — 7 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory, information-theory, llm — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch10-introduction]]: estimate expectations from draws of $g$ with weights $q/g$ via ratio estimator (10.3) (p. 265) (new)
- group: marginalisation ~ marginalization
  - `marginalisation` — 3 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[stone-2022-information/appf-rules]]: summing or integrating a joint distribution over one variable
  - `marginalization` — 5 digest(s); information-theory, llm — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch17-principles-p2]]: integrate the joint posterior over unwanted parameters, e.g. $p(A|DI)=\int dB\,p(AB|DI)$
- group: cofactor ~ q-factor
  - `cofactor` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch05-determinants]]: $C_{ij}=(-1)^{i+j}\det$ of A with row i and column j removed
  - `q-factor` — 6 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bertsekas-2026-rlcourse/ch01-exact-p1]]: $g_k(x_k,u_k)+J^*_{k+1}(f_k(x_k,u_k))$; also "action value" or "Q-value"
- group: tipping-point ~ tipping-point-k
  - `tipping-point` — 6 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[mitchell-2009-guidedtour/ch06-genetics]]: Mitchell's quoted label for the burst of progress in genetics that began with the 1953 double helix
  - `tipping-point-k` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch09-collective-p1]]: the number of contributors $k$ needed. Pressure to contribute rises with $k/n$
- group: bass-model ~ ras-model
  - `bass-model` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[rogers-2003-diffusion/ch05-innovation-p3]]: aggregate forecasting model with media (p), interpersonal (q) and market potential (m) parameters
  - `ras-model` — 5 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch01-introduction]]: only prefigured here: ideas "reach" people and are "found acceptable" before being used. The model is not named or stated in this unit
- group: liapunov-function ~ lyapunov-function
  - `liapunov-function` — 3 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[strogatz-2024-nonlinear/ch07-limit-p1]]: positive definite $V$ with $\dot V<0$ away from $\mathbf x^*$
  - `lyapunov-function` — 3 digest(s); control-theory, information-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch05-dynamic]]: energy-like $V$ with $V>0$, $\dot V\le0$ (or $<0$) near equilibrium
- group: link-function ~ risk-function
  - `link-function` — 4 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch16-generalized]]: maps the mean to the linear predictor scale; canonical link = the one in the exponential-family exponent
  - `risk-function` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch13-decision]]: sampling-theory expected loss R_j(S) or R_α for fixed true state
- group: mediation ~ mediator
  - `mediation` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch00-introduction]]: splitting an effect into direct and indirect parts via a mediator
  - `mediator` — 5 digest(s); bayesian, decision-game-theory — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch04-confounding]]: variable on the causal path from X to Y; controlling for it blocks the effect
- group: natural-direct-effect ~ natural-indirect-effect
  - `natural-direct-effect` — 3 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2016-primer/ch04-counterfactuals]]: $E[Y_{1,M_0}-Y_{0,M_0}]$ (new)
  - `natural-indirect-effect` — 3 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2016-primer/ch04-counterfactuals]]: $E[Y_{0,M_1}-Y_{0,M_0}]$ (new)
- group: supervised-learning ~ unsupervised-learning
  - `supervised-learning` — 3 digest(s); llm, political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch19-classifying]]: fit a model to labelled training texts, then predict labels for unlabelled texts (p. 318) (new)
  - `unsupervised-learning` — 2 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch01-introduction]]: build a model from inputs without output labels, to describe the data's structure
- group: coase-theorem ~ coxs-theorem
  - `coase-theorem` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch27-law]]: with no transaction costs (and small stakes), resources flow to highest-valued use whoever holds the right
  - `coxs-theorem` — 3 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch15-paradoxes]]: the finite-set derivation of the rules; Jaynes accepts well-defined limits
- group: convolution ~ deconvolution
  - `convolution` — 3 digest(s); information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch07-matrix]]: $c_k=\sum_{i+j=k+1}a_ib_j$; coefficients of a polynomial product
  - `deconvolution` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch46-deconvolution]]: recovering the image from data blurred by a known operator and corrupted by noise
- group: dimension-of-a-theory ~ dimension-of-theory
  - `dimension-of-a-theory` — 3 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/appviii-untitled]]: $d_F(a)$, the number of parameters, equal to the dimension of the favourable possibilities
  - `dimension-of-theory` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/appii-following]]: number $d$ such that no d-tuple of field statements clashes with the theory, but a d-tuple plus the theory sorts the rest into falsifiers…
- group: doubly-robust-estimation ~ doubly-robust-estimator
  - `doubly-robust-estimation` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch13-standardization]]: consistent if either the treatment model or the outcome model is correct
  - `doubly-robust-estimator` — 3 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch14-g]]: consistent if either the model for $E[H\mid L]$ or both treatment and censoring models are correct
- group: functional-equation ~ functional-equation-g
  - `functional-equation` — 3 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch01-i-p1]]: an equation in which the unknown function appears on both sides, such as (8.1)
  - `functional-equation-g` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[strogatz-2024-nonlinear/ch10-one-p2]]: g(x) = αg²(x/α), g(0)=1, g′(0)=0, describing the limit at the onset of chaos
- group: logical-improbability ~ logical-probability
  - `logical-improbability` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch07-simplicity]]: prior improbability. Popper ties it to high testability, against Jeffreys
  - `logical-probability` — 3 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch06-degrees]]: probability due to logical form; complementary to falsifiability; not statistical probability
- group: reification ~ verification
  - `reification` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch05-traditional]]: treating meanings as individual things
  - `verification` — 3 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch03-people]]: nature reveals to the principal whether x is better, with probability $v$. It makes false signals ineffective but does not penalize them
- group: truth-function ~ truth-functional
  - `truth-function` — 3 digest(s); analytic-philosophy, information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch51-appendices]]: $\mathbb{1}[S]=1$ if $S$ is true, else 0
  - `truth-functional` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch05-connectives]]: named here only as a property *because* lacks; not explained in this unit
- group: vector-space ~ vector-state
  - `vector-space` — 3 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch01-vector]]: a set with addition and scalar multiplication over $\mathbf{F}$ satisfying the six rule-groups of 1.20; elements may be lists, sequences …
  - `vector-state` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch03-determinate]]: a state with several components, treated as one operand
- group: censoring ~ centering
  - `censoring` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch08-selection]]: loss to follow-up before the outcome is observed ($C=1$); the analysis then uses the uncensored
  - `centering` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch04-linear-p2]]: subtracting the mean of a predictor; makes α the expected outcome at the average predictor and decorrelates α and β here
- group: compatibilism ~ compatibility
  - `compatibilism` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch10-big]]: the clash between free will and determinism is only apparent, a clash between two levels of description
  - `compatibility` — 2 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[rogers-2003-diffusion/ch06-attributes-p1]]: perceived consistency with values, past experience and needs
- group: complete-information-game ~ incomplete-information-game
  - `complete-information-game` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch08-democratic-p2]]: each player knows everything about the game, including types and preferences
  - `incomplete-information-game` — 2 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch08-democratic-p2]]: a player is uncertain of the other's type, here whether the opposition is weak or strong
- group: explicit-regularization ~ implicit-regularization
  - `explicit-regularization` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch09-regularization]]: adding a penalty $\lambda g[\phi]$ to the loss to favor certain parameters
  - `implicit-regularization` — 2 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch09-regularization]]: bias of GD/SGD toward certain solutions (p. 141), which can be interpreted as extra loss terms
- group: extension ~ intension
  - `extension` — 2 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch10-truth]]: what a term applies to in a given world
  - `intension` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch10-truth]]: function from worlds to extensions (to individuals, for singular terms); sometimes from intensions to intensions
- group: inference ~ reference
  - `inference` — 2 digest(s); llm, political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch22-principles]]: using a sample to estimate a quantity in a well-defined, possibly counterfactual, population
  - `reference` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch02-feedback]]: the signal $r$ giving the desired output
- group: logical-vs-causal-dependence ~ logical-vs-causal-independence
  - `logical-vs-causal-dependence` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch03-elementary]]: probabilities express logical connection, which runs both ways in time; physical influence runs only forward
  - `logical-vs-causal-independence` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch04-elementary]]: independence is a property of the robot's probabilities, not of events
- group: resource-bias ~ source-bias
  - `resource-bias` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch04-selecting]]: texts often better reflect populations with more resources to produce, record and store them
  - `source-bias` — 2 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[rogers-2003-diffusion/ch02-history-p3]]: sponsor-serving priorities in marketing diffusion research
- group: uniquely-decodable-code ~ uniquely-decodeable-code
  - `uniquely-decodable-code` — 2 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch05-data-p1]]: code whose extension is non-singular; same Kraft constraint as prefix codes (McMillan)
  - `uniquely-decodeable-code` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch05-symbol]]: distinct source strings always have distinct encodings under $C^+$
- group: ambivalence ~ bivalence
  - `ambivalence` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[zaller-1992-massopinion/ch04-coming]]: holding considerations on both sides of an issue; measured by paired opposing remarks, ambivalence codes and star codes
  - `bivalence` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch02-scope]]: every case makes each sentence true or false, never both
- group: biconditional ~ conditional
  - `biconditional` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch05-connectives]]: ↔; *A iff B*; both directions of the conditional
  - `conditional` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch05-connectives]]: →; *If A, then B* or *A only if B*; A is the antecedent, B the consequent; says nothing causal
- group: bilinear-functional ~ linear-functional
  - `bilinear-functional` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch09-multilinear-p2]]: function $V\times W\to\mathbf F$ linear in each slot. Space $\mathcal B(V,W)$ has dimension $(\dim V)(\dim W)$
  - `linear-functional` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p3]]: a linear map V → 𝐅
- group: cancelability ~ cancellability
  - `cancelability` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch13-implicative]]: a speaker can forestall an implicature without contradiction
  - `cancellability` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[grice-1975-conversation/main]]: a generalized conversational implicature can be cancelled, explicitly or contextually, by opting out of the CP
- group: cardinal-payoff ~ ordinal-payoff
  - `cardinal-payoff` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch08-democratic-p3]]: payoffs showing how much more one outcome is valued; needed for incomplete information games
  - `ordinal-payoff` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch01-introduction]]: payoff numbers matter only through their order. Any increasing transformation represents the same preferences
- group: cayley-graph ~ paley-graph
  - `cayley-graph` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch07-cayley]]: vertices are group elements; a~a∘s for s in an inverse-closed S
  - `paley-graph` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch07-cayley]]: Cayley graph on Z/p, p≡1 mod 4, generators = nonzero squares
- group: coarse-coding ~ sparse-coding
  - `coarse-coding` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sutton-2018-rl/ch09-policy-p1]]: overlapping receptive fields; size and shape control generalization, number of features controls acuity
  - `sparse-coding` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch28-latent-p2]]: MAP $Z$ for fixed $W$ under Laplace prior and Gaussian noise; dictionary learning estimates $W$
- group: collective ~ connective
  - `collective` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch08-probability-p1]]: von Mises: a sequence, indefinitely continuable, obeying the axioms of convergence and randomness
  - `connective` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch05-connectives]]: an expression that forms a new sentence from old ones; TFL has five
- group: compartment-model ~ compartmental-model
  - `compartment-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch04-examples]]: body or system split into perfectly mixed compartments with flows proportional to concentration differences
  - `compartmental-model` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[shiller-2019-narrative/app-applying]]: population divided into states (S, I, R, E…) with flow equations
- group: computation-graph ~ computational-graph
  - `computation-graph` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch05-vector]]: graph of intermediate variables and elementary operations
  - `computational-graph` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch07-gradients]]: acyclic graph of operations; backpropagation still applies to it
- group: concept-drift ~ concept-shift
  - `concept-drift` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch23-prediction]]: the predictor–outcome relationship changes over time, so historical fit misleads; successful predictions can cause it
  - `concept-shift` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch19-beyond]]: $p(y|x)$ changes (annotation shift); not fixable without labeled target data
- group: conclusion ~ inclusion
  - `conclusion` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch01-arguments]]: the sentence the premises are meant to support; marked by "therefore"
  - `inclusion` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch05-democracy]]: Dahl's dimension: who gets to participate (p. 150) (new)
- group: constitution-principle ~ constitutive-principle
  - `constitution-principle` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[bai-2022-constitutional/main]]: a natural-language rule; for SL, a critique request plus a revision request; for RL, a choice instruction
  - `constitutive-principle` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[kuhn-1962-structure/ch10-revolutions]]: a law that once was empirical becomes a rule no single measurement could upset, as with constant proportion after Dalton
- group: cross-covariance ~ cross-covariance-d
  - `cross-covariance` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch01-symbols]]: same with $\mathbf y$ in the second factor
  - `cross-covariance-d` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch14-general]]: $\mathbf D_{k+1}$, covariance of $(\mathbf x_k,\mathbf x_{k+1})$ given $\mathbf y_{1:k}$. It gives the smoother gain $\mathbf G_k=\mathbf…
- group: d-adic-distribution ~ dyadic-distribution
  - `d-adic-distribution` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch05-data-p1]]: all probabilities are powers $D^{-n}$; exactly the case where $L=H_D$ is achievable
  - `dyadic-distribution` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch05-data-p2]]: pmf with $\log\frac1{p(x)}$ an integer for all $x$
- group: dependent-mixed-system ~ independent-mixed-system
  - `dependent-mixed-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p3]]: mixed member proportional (MMP): list tier compensates for constituency disproportionality
  - `independent-mixed-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p3]]: mixed parallel system: the two components run independently
- group: discount-parameter ~ discount-parameter-w
  - `discount-parameter` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch01-problemof]]: $w$, the weight of the next move relative to the current one; reflects both impatience and the chance the relationship ends
  - `discount-parameter-w` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch07-promote]]: value of the next move's payoff relative to the current one; reflects chance of ending plus impatience
- group: efficiency-estimator ~ efficient-estimator
  - `efficiency-estimator` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch04-asymptotics]]: optimal MSE divided by the estimator's MSE
  - `efficient-estimator` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch12-information-p3]]: an unbiased estimator that meets the Cramér–Rao bound with equality
- group: equivalence-class ~ equivalence-laws
  - `equivalence-class` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2016-primer/ch02-graphical]]: graphs with the same skeleton and v-structures, hence the same testable implications
  - `equivalence-laws` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[magnus-2025-forallx/ch100-quick]]: ⇔ laws (DN, DeM, Dist…) for rewriting in chains
- group: exclusive-collective-good ~ inclusive-collective-good
  - `exclusive-collective-good` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[olson-1965-collectiveaction/ch01-theory-p2]]: benefit fixed in supply (e.g. supracompetitive price), so members want fewer others
  - `inclusive-collective-good` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[olson-1965-collectiveaction/ch01-theory-p2]]: benefit expands with the group, so members welcome new members
- group: explicature ~ implicature
  - `explicature` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch13-implicative]]: in later Relevance Theory, what is explicitly conveyed rather than merely implicated; in later use understood more vaguely than at first
  - `implicature` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch13-topic]]: implying credit or responsibility without literally claiming it
- group: explicit-representation ~ implicit-representation
  - `explicit-representation` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch06-styles]]: a physically structured token with an interpretation, plus a mechanism for reading it
  - `implicit-representation` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[dennett-1987-intentional-stance/ch06-styles]]: information logically implied by what is stored explicitly; not the same as "potentially explicit"
- group: finite-mixture-model ~ infinite-mixture-model
  - `finite-mixture-model` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch22-finite]]: $p(y_i)=\sum_h\lambda_hf(y_i|\theta_h)$, a hierarchical model with latent component indicators
  - `infinite-mixture-model` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch34-independent]]: mixture with unbounded number of clusters, possibly hierarchical; most of the cited ideas build on the Dirichlet process
- group: heteroscedastic-regression ~ heteroskedastic-regression
  - `heteroscedastic-regression` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch05-loss]]: regression where the predicted variance depends on the input
  - `heteroskedastic-regression` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch16-deep]]: regression where the predicted variance depends on the input
- group: inductive-scepticism ~ inductive-skepticism
  - `inductive-scepticism` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch03-induction]]: Hume's view that induction is natural but has no rational basis
  - `inductive-skepticism` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch04-popper]]: Popper's view that no support except deductive logic exists; confirmation is a myth
- group: inferential-theory-of-meaning ~ referential-theory-of-meaning
  - `inferential-theory-of-meaning` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch06-use]]: Lycan's name for a view built on Sellars' entry, exit and language–language rules, with inferring as a social act
  - `referential-theory-of-meaning` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[lycan-2019-language/ch01-introduction]]: an expression means what it stands for; understanding is knowing the referents
- group: log-link ~ logit-link
  - `log-link` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch09-big]]: maps positive parameters to the linear scale; the inverse is exponentiation
  - `logit-link` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[mcelreath-2020-rethinking/ch09-big]]: $\text{logit}(p)=\log\frac{p}{1-p}$; its inverse is the logistic
- group: max-pooling ~ mean-pooling
  - `max-pooling` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch10-learning]]: keep the maximum of each group of outputs; nonlinear dimension reduction
  - `mean-pooling` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch13-graph]]: averaging output node embeddings into one graph vector
- group: occam-factor ~ ockham-factor
  - `occam-factor` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch28-model]]: the factor below one multiplying the best-fit likelihood. In 1-D with a uniform prior it is $\sigma_{w|D}/\sigma_w$; in $K$ dimensions $P…
  - `ockham-factor` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch20-model]]: $W=\int d\theta\,(L/L_{\max})\,p(\theta|MI)\le1$
- group: pbh-test ~ psi-test
  - `pbh-test` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch14-fundamental]]: rank test of $(A-sI\ \ B)$ for reachability or stabilizability
  - `psi-test` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch09-repetitive-p2]]: ψ_B, the exact max decibel support for any alternative in the Bernoulli class
- group: policy-rl ~ policy-rule
  - `policy-rl` — 1 digest(s); llm — e.g. [[prince-2023-udl/ch19-reinforcement]]: rule giving action probabilities per state; stochastic or deterministic
  - `policy-rule` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[taylor-1993-discretion/main]]: systematic contingency plan lasting a reasonably long time. It is not necessarily a fixed setting or a mechanical formula
- group: prediction-function ~ production-function
  - `prediction-function` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[griffiths-2006-optimal/main]]: the posterior median of $t_{total}$ as a function of $t$
  - `production-function` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch09-collective-p1]]: how many contributors the group goal requires
- group: probability-as-frequency ~ probability-vs-frequency
  - `probability-as-frequency` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch09-incessant]]: probability defined operationally as long-run frequency; constancy tested by successive estimates
  - `probability-vs-frequency` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch09-repetitive-p2]]: probability is an assigned state of knowledge; frequency is a measured fact; they are equal only for particular knowledge states
- group: probit-regression ~ robit-regression
  - `probit-regression` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch15-generalized]]: thresholded latent Gaussian; gives EM and Gibbs samplers
  - `robit-regression` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch17-models]]: binary regression with latent $t_\nu$ errors, $\nu$ fixed low
- group: punctuated-equilibria ~ punctuated-equilibrium
  - `punctuated-equilibria` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[mitchell-2009-guidedtour/ch05-evolution]]: long stasis broken by short bursts of change and new species
  - `punctuated-equilibrium` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[godfreysmith-2003-theory/ch06-kuhn]]: long stasis broken by rapid, disorderly change; compared with Kuhn's pattern
- group: realization ~ retaliation
  - `realization` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch09-transfer-p1]]: a state-space system $(A,B,C,D)$ with $G(s)=C(sI-A)^{-1}B+D$; not unique; minimal has fewest states
  - `retaliation` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[axelrod-1984-cooperation/ch02-success]]: immediate defection after an "uncalled for" defection by the other
- group: reference-price ~ reference-prior
  - `reference-price` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch07-bargains]]: the price one would normally expect to pay
  - `reference-prior` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch03-statistics-p2]]: maximizes expected KL (mutual information) between prior and posterior; Jeffreys in 1-d
- group: resampling ~ sampling
  - `resampling` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch11-particle]]: draw $N$ indices from the weights, reset weights to $1/N$; unbiased, adds variance
  - `sampling` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch06-linear]]: exact discrete-time model for piecewise-constant input: $\Phi=e^{Ah}$, $\Gamma=(\int_0^he^{As}ds)B$
- group: shared-risk ~ shared-task
  - `shared-risk` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[schelling-1960-conflict/ch08-threat]]: a recognizable probability of mutual disaster imposed on both sides, which the other side can reduce by complying or withdrawing, and ide…
  - `shared-task` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch22-principles]]: evaluating methods by comparing performance on one dataset. Less useful for causal methods
- group: social-bot ~ social-set
  - `social-bot` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[lazer-2018-fakenews/main]]: automated account impersonating a human
  - `social-set` — 1 digest(s); communication-theory — e.g. [[lippmann-1922-publicopinion/ch03-contact]]: group whose approval matters most to us; filters ideas and fixes admissible sources
- group: static-stability ~ static-viability
  - `static-stability` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[meadows-2008-thinking/ch03-systems]]: constancy of a system's condition over time; visible, and not the same as resilience
  - `static-viability` — 1 digest(s); complex-systems — e.g. [[schelling-1971-segregation/main-p2]]: a mixture inside both curves, where all present of both colors are content
- group: suboptimalization ~ suboptimization
  - `suboptimalization` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch05-v]]: the critical level of each item depends only on its own marginal demand distribution, despite correlation
  - `suboptimization` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[meadows-2008-thinking/ch03-systems]]: a subsystem's goals dominate at the cost of the whole system's goals
- group: supervised-learning-text ~ unsupervised-learning-text
  - `supervised-learning-text` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2013-textasdata/main]]: learn word–category relationship from a hand-coded training set; classify the rest
  - `unsupervised-learning-text` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2013-textasdata/main]]: methods that estimate categories and assignments without predefined labels
- group: surprisal ~ surprise
  - `surprisal` — 1 digest(s); theory-of-mind — e.g. [[frank-2012-rsa/main]]: $-\log p(x)$, how much information is gained by observing $x$; the measure of informativeness
  - `surprise` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch06-norms]]: the sign that something violates an active expectation or what the model treats as normal
- group: switching-function ~ switching-function-k
  - `switching-function` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[liberzon-2012-calculus/ch04-maximum-p2]]: $\varphi=\langle p^*,g(x^*)\rangle$ whose sign determines $u^*$
  - `switching-function-k` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bellman-1957-dp/ch08-viii]]: $K_i(t)$, the first-variation coefficients; $K_i>K_j$ forces $\varphi_i=1$

## 4. Containment within a shared field (798 contained ids)

Each group: the shorter id, then the longer ids (same field) that contain it.

- contained: `matrix` in 90 id(s)
  - `matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: rectangular array of numbers with two indices; size $m\times n$
  - `adjacency-matrix` — 24 digest(s); linear-algebra, llm — e.g. [[bonacich-1987-power/main]]: R, with R_ij=1 if a relation exists and zero diagonal; powers Rᵏ count paths
  - `associative-law-matrix-product` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch01-vectors]]: (AB)C = A(BC), proved column by column
  - `augmented-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch03-four-p2]]: $[A\ b]$, b added as a column so row operations act on both sides
  - `banded-sparse-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch02-solving-p2]]: mostly zeros near a diagonal band; tridiagonal K is fast to solve
  - `block-diagonal-link-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[bryan-2006-eigenvector/main]]: the form of $A$ for disconnected subwebs
  - `block-diagonal-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch08-operators]]: square blocks on the diagonal, 0 elsewhere
  - `block-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: matrix whose entries are matrices; blocks must fit in height and width
  - `block-matrix-multiplication` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: blocks multiply by the scalar rule, with factor order preserved when the blocks conform
  - `chain-rule-derivative-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: $Dh(z)=Df(g(z))Dg(z)$; for scalar f, $\nabla h(z)=Dg(z)^T\nabla f(g(z))$
  - `circulant-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: a combination of powers of P; diagonalized by F
  - `companion-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: turns a scalar higher-order equation into u′ = Au
  - `confusion-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch14-least]]: table of counts of (true label, predicted label) pairs; $2\times2$ or $K\times K$
  - `covariance-matrix` — 6 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch06-probability-p1]]: $\mathbb E[\boldsymbol{xx}^\top]-\mathbb E[\boldsymbol x]\mathbb E[\boldsymbol x]^\top$; symmetric positive semidefinite
  - `defective-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch04-matrix-p1]]: fewer than $n$ independent eigenvectors; not diagonalizable
  - `diagonal-triangular-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: zero off the diagonal / zero above or below it
  - `diffusion-matrix-exponential` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch10-random]]: continuous-time limit exp(t(W − I))
  - `dilation-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch04-adjacency]]: $\begin{pmatrix}0&B\\B^T&0\end{pmatrix}$ built from $B$
  - `doubly-stochastic-matrix` — 2 digest(s); information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch02-entropy]]: rows and columns sum to 1; iff the uniform distribution is stationary
  - `elimination-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch02-solving-p1]]: E_ij: I with −ℓ_ij in position (i,j); E = E₃₂E₃₁E₂₁
  - `feature-matrix-design-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch09-linear]]: $\Phi\in\mathbb R^{N\times K}$ with $\Phi_{ij}=\phi_j(x_i)$
  - `first-difference-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch15-multi]]: $D$ with rows $(e_{i+1}-e_i)^T$; $\|Dx\|^2$ penalizes roughness
  - `flop-count-matrix-ops` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: dense $Ax$ costs about $2mn$ flops; sparse costs between $\mathbf{nnz}(A)$ and $2\,\mathbf{nnz}(A)$
  - `flop-count-matrix-product` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: about 2mnp flops for dense AB; sparse and triple-product variants
  - `fourier-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: $F_N$ with $w^{jk}$ in row j, column k; eigenvectors of P_N; $\bar F^TF=NI$
  - `fourier-matrix-and-circulant` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch08-linear]]: F diagonalizes P and every circulant matrix; Fx is a discrete Fourier transform
  - `gaussian-random-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch14-approximating]]: matrix of i.i.d. $\mathcal N(0,1/d)$ entries; $Rx$ has independent $\mathcal N(0,\|x\|^2/d)$ entries
  - `generalized-laplacian-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch26-planar]]: $M=L+D$: negative on edges, zero on non-edges, free diagonal
  - `google-matrix-mixing` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[bryan-2006-eigenvector/main]]: $M=(1-m)A+mS$; weighted average of link matrix and uniform matrix, $m=0.15$ reportedly
  - `google-pagerank-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/app1-ranks]]: G = αA + (1−α)/N with column-normalized links, all entries positive
  - `gram-matrix` — 5 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: $G=A^TA$, entries $a_i^Ta_j$, symmetric
  - `hermitian-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: S̄ᵀ = S; real eigenvalues, orthogonal eigenvectors
  - `hessenberg-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: one nonzero subdiagonal; the near-triangular form from Step 1
  - `hessian-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch09-linear]]: symmetric matrix S of second derivatives ∂²F/∂xᵢ∂xⱼ
  - `hilbert-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch08-linear]]: BᵀB for the powers 1, x, x², …, with entries 1/(i+j+1)
  - `identity-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: $I_{ij}=1$ if $i=j$, else 0; $Ix=x$; columns are unit vectors
  - `incidence-matrix` — 10 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch07-matrix]]: $n\times m$ matrix of a directed graph, $\pm1$ entries per edge; flows to net node flows
  - `inverse-matrix` — 10 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p2]]: square A with AB=BA=I for some square B; unique (p. 91). Author does not mention determinants in this unit.
  - `jacobian-derivative-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/app-appendices]]: $m\times n$ matrix $Df(z)_{ij}=\partial f_i/\partial x_j$; rows are $\nabla f_i^T$
  - `jacobian-matrix` — 2 digest(s); complex-systems, linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch18-nonlinear]]: the $m\times n$ matrix $Df(x)$ of partial derivatives of $f$; gradient of $\|f\|^2$ is $2Df^Tf$

  - `second-difference-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch02-solving-p2]]: K, T, B built from the −1, 2, −1 pattern with fixed or free ends
  - `selector-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch07-matrix]]: each row is a transposed unit vector, so $Ax$ copies chosen entries
  - `signed-adjacency-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch44-bipartite]]: symmetric ±1 matrix with the nonzero pattern of A

  - `sparse-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: few nonzeros; $\mathbf{nnz}(A)$, density $\mathbf{nnz}(A)/(mn)$, no precise cutoff
  - `substochastic-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[bryan-2006-eigenvector/main]]: column sums $\le1$; arises with dangling nodes; 1 need not be an eigenvalue
  - `symmetric-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: square $A$ with $A=A^T$

  - `toeplitz-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch07-matrix]]: entries constant along diagonals; $T(a)$ has shifted copies of $a$ as columns
  - `triangular-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch04-matrix-p1]]: zeros on one side of the diagonal; determinant = product of the diagonal
  - `unitary-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[strang-2023-introduction/ch06-eigenvalues-p3]]: Q̄ᵀ = Q⁻¹; eigenvalues with |λ| = 1

  - `vandermonde-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: $A$ in (6.7); $Ac$ evaluates a polynomial at many points
  - `walk-matrix` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch01-introduction]]: $W_G=M_GD_G^{-1}$, one diffusion step; lazy version $I/2+W_G/2$
- contained: `model` in 77 id(s)
  - `model` — 4 digest(s); control-theory, decision-game-theory, linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch06-black]]: usually a homomorphism of the system modelled; higher on the lattice = more realistic
  - `admixture-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch01-introduction]]: a model where each document is proportionally assigned to different categories
  - `attention-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/app-ch02]]: decision-theoretic model in which a principal pays to learn alternatives' locations
  - `auto-regressive-model` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch13-least]]: predict z_{t+1} linearly from the previous M values
  - `autoregressive-model` — 2 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch22-autoregressive]]: model $p(x_{1:T})=\prod_t p(x_t|x_{1:t-1})$ with a restricted form for each conditional
  - `black-and-white-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[converse-1964-beliefsystems/ch03-sections]]: a perfectly stable hard core plus randomly responding remainder, with no gray area. A "third force" of converts explains departures
  - `calvert-leader-reputation-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[shepsle-2010-analyzing/ch14-leadership]]: repeated leader-follower game with private information on punishment cost
  - `cheap-talk-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch03-people]]: Crawford–Sobel-style costless messages. Here modified so that the principal is uncertain about the speaker's knowledge ($k$) and interest…
  - `clutter-mixture-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch05-modeling]]: Gaussian + uniform mixture with detection probability $\rho$
  - `compartment-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch04-examples]]: body or system split into perfectly mixed compartments with flows proportional to concentration differences
  - `constitutional-review-models` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch15-institutional-p3]]: American (decentralized, a posteriori, concrete) versus European (centralized, abstract and/or concrete, broad standing)
  - `design-v-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch15-architecture-p1]]: design pattern with requirements→components on the left leg and implementation→validation on the right
  - `directed-graphical-model` — 2 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch08-when]]: a joint factorized as ∏ p(x_k | Pa_k) (8.31)
  - `distribution-vs-sample-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sutton-2018-rl/ch08-planning]]: full outcome probabilities vs one sampled outcome; sample models are easier to get
  - `expectancy-value-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[chong-2007-framing/main]]: attitude $=\sum v_i w_i$, evaluations weighted by salience, $\sum w_i=1$
  - `flow-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch03-system-p2]]: continuous-flow approximation of a discrete queue
  - `fotd-model` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch11-pid]]: first-order-plus-time-delay model $Ke^{-\tau s}/(1+sT)$
  - `framework-vs-model` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[ostrom-1990-commons/ch06-framework]]: a framework lists variables and questions. A model gives precise predictions by fixing variables
  - `gaussian-mixture-model` — 3 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p2]]: diagonal-covariance mixture with Dirichlet, normal-gamma priors
  - `generalized-additive-model` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch13-least]]: ŷ as a sum of piecewise-linear functions of single features
  - `generalized-linear-model` — 4 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch09-linear]]: $y=\sigma(\theta^\top\phi(x))$ with an activation $\sigma$; building block of feedforward networks
  - `hidden-markov-model` — 8 digest(s); bayesian, control-theory, information-theory, llm — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch04-entropy]]: here, $Y_i=\phi(X_i)$ for a Markov chain $X$; bounded entropy rate
  - `hierarchical-model` — 30 digest(s); bayesian, political-science — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p2]]: ARD and LDA both place priors on parameters of lower-level distributions
  - `internal-model-control` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch15-architecture-p1]]: model in parallel with the process, estimating the disturbance effect; cancels process poles/zeros
  - `internal-model-principle` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch08-output]]: the controller contains a model of the process (and its environment) (p. 8-13) (new)

  - `two-degree-of-freedom-controller` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch08-output]]: observer + state feedback + trajectory generator (feedforward); reference and disturbance responses decoupled (p. 8-23, 8-24) (new)
  - `two-degrees-of-freedom` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch02-feedback]]: controller with different transfer functions $C_{ur}$ and $C_{uy}$
- contained: `delay` in 3 id(s)
  - `delay` — 6 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch08-transmission]]: here a device repeating its input one step later (p. 147–148). Also the slow transfer of variety when part of the sender acts only throug…
  - `delay-dead-time` — 18 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch12-error]]: R cannot complete its action before T's outcome starts
  - `delay-margin` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch10-frequency]]: smallest additional time delay that destabilises the loop
  - `information-delay-bias` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[meadows-2008-thinking/ch07-living]]: biased, late or missing information makes feedback loops malfunction
- contained: `derivative` in 3 id(s)
  - `derivative` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch05-vector]]: limit of the difference quotient; slope of the tangent
  - `chain-rule-derivative-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: $Dh(z)=Df(g(z))Dg(z)$; for scalar f, $\nabla h(z)=Dg(z)^T\nabla f(g(z))$
  - `jacobian-derivative-matrix` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/app-appendices]]: $m\times n$ matrix $Df(z)_{ij}=\partial f_i/\partial x_j$; rows are $\nabla f_i^T$
  - `partial-derivative` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch05-vector]]: derivative with respect to one variable, others held fixed
- contained: `deterrence` in 3 id(s)
  - `deterrence` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[schelling-1960-conflict/ch01-retarded]]: influencing another's choices by shaping his expectation of how we will respond
  - `deterrence-compellence` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch06-strategic]]: deterrence stops an action; compellence forces one
  - `stable-deterrence` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[schelling-1960-conflict/ch10-surprise]]: a balance in which neither side, striking first, can destroy the other's ability to retaliate
  - `third-area-deterrence` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[schelling-1960-conflict/ch10-surprise]]: deterring aggression in a region outside the two main powers by a threat more credible than mutual suicide
- contained: `econs` in 3 id(s)
  - `econs` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch05-california]]: term used in passing and not defined in this chapter; Thaler's early paper title implicitly suggested that only economists behave like Econs
  - `econ-vs-human` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch01-supposedly]]: Econ: a fictional perfect optimizer with unbiased beliefs; Human: a real person who departs from it
  - `econs-vs-humans` — 4 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance — e.g. [[kahneman-2011-thinking/ch34-frames]]: Econs have reality-bound preferences; Humans' are frame-bound
  - `humans-vs-econs` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch32-going]]: Humans make predictable errors; Econs already do the right thing
- contained: `electoral-system` in 3 id(s)
  - `electoral-system` — 2 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p1]]: laws and regulations governing electoral competition, including formula, ballot structure and district magnitude
  - `electoral-system-choice` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p3]]: adoption as outcome of party self-interest (policy or office), general interest, precedent, external pressure, accident
  - `majoritarian-electoral-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p1]]: system where those with the most votes win, whether by absolute or relative majority
  - `mixed-electoral-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch13-elections-p3]]: majoritarian and proportional formulas combined in the same election; tiers not required
- contained: `enlightenment` in 3 id(s)
  - `enlightenment` — 4 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch04-people]]: a speaker's words or actions raise the listener's ability to predict accurately; no truth is required
  - `enlightenment-deception` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch01-knowledge]]: enlightenment: others give us knowledge. Deception: testimony reduces our ability to predict consequences
  - `enlightenment-vs-persuasion` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch10-institutions]]: persuasion and enlightenment are not the same, so deliberation is far more likely to be beneficial where conditions for enlightenment hold
  - `persuasion-enlightenment-deception` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[lupia-1998-dilemma/ch07-laboratory-p1]]: persuasion = principal's prediction matches the statement. Enlightened = matched and correct. Deceived = matched and incorrect
- contained: `exponential-family` in 3 id(s)
  - `exponential-family` — 8 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch12-information-p2]]: P*(a) ∝ Q(a)e^{λa²} and P_λ ∝ P₁^λP₂^{1−λ} are exponential tilts
  - `exponential-family-factor-analysis` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch28-latent-p1]]: natural parameters $\Theta=ZW$ with a non-Gaussian likelihood; needs approximate inference
  - `exponential-family-gibbs` — 6 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[cover-2006-elements/ch11-maximum]]: the max-entropy solution has the form $e^{\lambda_0+\sum\lambda_ir_i}$
  - `exponential-family-gibbs-distribution` — 10 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-1957-information/main]]: max-entropy solution $p_i=\exp\{-[\lambda_0+\sum\lambda_kf_k(x_i)]\}$
- contained: `flop-count` in 3 id(s)
  - `flop-count` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/app-appendices]]: approximate number of arithmetic operations, lower-order terms dropped
  - `flop-count-complexity` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch01-vectors]]: number of floating point operations of a specific method, simplified to leading order
  - `flop-count-matrix-ops` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch06-matrices]]: dense $Ax$ costs about $2mn$ flops; sparse costs between $\mathbf{nnz}(A)$ and $2\,\mathbf{nnz}(A)$
  - `flop-count-matrix-product` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: about 2mnp flops for dense AB; sparse and triple-product variants
- contained: `g-formula` in 3 id(s)
  - `g-formula` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch21-g]]: outcome mean standardized to the confounder distribution, each factor conditioned on past treatment and covariate history
  - `big-g-formula` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch21-g]]: g-formula using all variables, measured and unmeasured
  - `parametric-g-formula` — 2 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch13-standardization]]: plug-in g-formula estimator that uses parametric models for the conditional outcome
  - `plug-in-g-formula` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch21-g]]: g-formula with estimated components; parametric if the models are parametric

  - `latent-gaussian-process-model` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch21-gaussian]]: GP on latent $f$ linked to a non-Gaussian likelihood; inference by GP-specific MCMC or Laplace/EP/VI
  - `logistic-gaussian-process` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch21-gaussian]]: density prior from exponentiating and normalizing a GP draw

  - `automorphism-group` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch39-testing]]: isomorphisms of a graph to itself; commutative here and embeds in $\{\pm1\}^n\cong(\mathbb Z/2)^n$
  - `group-representative-centroid` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch04-clustering]]: an $n$-vector $z_j$ per group; the best choice for fixed groups is the group mean
  - `hyperoctahedral-group` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch42-quadrature]]: $\mathcal B(n)$: signed permutation matrices, a subgroup of $\mathcal O(n)$

  - `partial-identification-bounds` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch16-instrumental]]: instrument alone gives only upper and lower bounds on the average effect, often wide
  - `subspace-identification` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch29-state-p2]]: SVD/PCA-based spectral initialization of LDS parameters
- contained: `incentive-compatibility` in 3 id(s)
  - `incentive-compatibility` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch13-incentives]]: the scheme must give the worker enough incentive to put in the desired effort; the text names it together with participation without defi…
  - `incentive-compatibility-constraint` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch08-interpreting]]: the screener's constraint that each type prefers the option designed for it, so that no type defects
  - `incentive-compatibility-revelation` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch15-workouts]]: participation and incentive-compatibility constraints in the fare example
  - `incentive-compatibility-revelation-principle` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[dixit-2008-art-strategy/ch10-auctions]]: truth-telling is optimal; Vickrey has bidders bid their true value (p. 284); the revelation principle itself is not discussed in this unit

  - `invariant` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[ashby-1956-cybernetics/ch05-stability]]: a statement that stays true while the system changes

  - `invariant-set` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[bertsekas-2026-rlcourse/ch01-exact-p5]]: $X$ with $\forall x\in X\ \exists u\in U(x): f(x,u)\in X$
  - `linear-time-invariant-system` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[astrom-2021-feedbacksystems/ch03-system-p1]]: superposition holds; response does not depend on when the input is applied

  - `lasso-ridge-shrinkage` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch18-variable]]: penalties pulling coefficients toward zero; the lasso can set some exactly to zero. This is not the hierarchical-model shrinkage of the c…

  - `gf-q-ldpc-code` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch47-low]]: LDPC code over a finite field GF($q$), decoded with a Fourier transform at cost $q\log q$
  - `irregular-ldpc-code` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch47-low]]: LDPC code whose nodes have unequal degrees
  - `turbo-and-ldpc-codes` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch11-error]]: semi-random constructions with iterative message-passing decoding
- contained: `logarithm` in 3 id(s)
  - `logarithm` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[stone-2022-information/appb-mathematical]]: $\log_a x$ is the power of $a$ that gives $x$. Logs turn products into sums.
  - `logarithm-as-bit-count` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[stone-2022-information/ch01-information]]: $n=\log_2 m$ is the number of binary decisions needed to choose among $m$ alternatives
  - `logarithm-base-2` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[pierce-1980-introduction/app-mathematical]]: the power of 2 that gives x; 2^(log x) = x
  - `natural-logarithm` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[pierce-1980-introduction/app-mathematical]]: base-e log, with log_e(1+x) ≈ x for small x
- contained: `marginalization` in 3 id(s)
  - `marginalization` — 5 digest(s); information-theory, llm — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch17-principles-p2]]: integrate the joint posterior over unwanted parameters, e.g. $p(A|DI)=\int dB\,p(AB|DI)$
  - `marginalization-paradox` — 3 digest(s); bayesian, information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/appb-mathematical]]: confusion arising from one symbol denoting probabilities conditional on different information
  - `nuisance-parameter-marginalization` — 3 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch04-elementary]]: integrate out parameters not of interest, as in (4.73)
  - `sum-rule-marginalization` — 2 digest(s); information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch06-probability-p1]]: $p(x)=\sum_y p(x,y)$ or $\int p(x,y)\,\mathrm dy$

  - `matrix-multiplication` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch02-linear-p1]]: $c_{ij}=\sum_l a_{il}b_{lj}$; needs matching inner dimensions; not commutative
  - `block-matrix-multiplication` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: blocks multiply by the scalar rule, with factor order preserved when the blocks conform
  - `matrix-multiplication-as-composition` — 11 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch03-linear-p1]]: here the product $ST$ of linear maps means "do $T$, then $S$"; no matrices yet
  - `matrix-multiplication-definition` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[boyd-2018-vmls/ch10-matrix]]: $C_{ij}=\sum_kA_{ik}B_{kj}$ for compatible sizes; the book also gives column, row, inner-product and outer-product readings
- contained: `mediation` in 3 id(s)
  - `mediation` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch00-introduction]]: splitting an effect into direct and indirect parts via a mediator
  - `mediation-direct-indirect-effect` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch08-counterfactuals]]: effect transmitted through a mediator vs not; introduced as counterfactual
  - `mediation-fallacy` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[pearl-2018-bookofwhy/ch09-mediation-p1]]: conditioning on a mediator rather than holding it constant. Harmless without mediator–outcome confounding
  - `mediation-formula` — 4 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[hernan-2020-whatif/ch23-causal]]: $\sum_m E[Y|A=1,M=m]\Pr[M=m|A=0]$; identifies the cross-world mean under assumptions
- contained: `methodological-rules` in 3 id(s)
  - `methodological-rules` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch02-problem]]: conventions governing how scientific statements are treated, e.g. keep testing, do not drop a tested hypothesis without good reason
  - `methodological-rule-neglect-extreme-improbability` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch08-probability-p3]]: decision not to explain reproducible effects as accumulations of accidents
  - `supreme-methodological-rule` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch02-problem]]: rules must not protect any statement from falsification
  - `uniformity-of-nature-as-methodological-rule` — 1 digest(s); analytic-philosophy — e.g. [[popper-1959-logic/ch10-corroboration]]: Popper reads the principle as the rule that new theories explain old corroborated regularities and that laws hold without exception
- contained: `mixture-model` in 3 id(s)
  - `mixture-model` — 5 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch11-density]]: convex combination of base densities with mixture weights $\pi_k$
  - `finite-mixture-model` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch22-finite]]: $p(y_i)=\sum_h\lambda_hf(y_i|\theta_h)$, a hierarchical model with latent component indicators
  - `gaussian-mixture-model` — 3 digest(s); bayesian, linear-algebra — e.g. [[blei-2017-variational/main-p2]]: diagonal-covariance mixture with Dirichlet, normal-gamma priors
  - `mixture-model-clustering` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[grimmer-2022-textasdata/ch12-clustering]]: generative model: draw cluster, then document from that cluster's distribution
- contained: `multiplicity` in 3 id(s)
  - `multiplicity` — 2 digest(s); information-theory, linear-algebra — e.g. [[axler-2024-ladr/ch08-operators]]: $\dim G(\lambda,T)$, the book's "algebraic multiplicity"
  - `algebraic-geometric-multiplicity` — 2 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[deisenroth-2020-mml/ch04-matrix-p1]]: root multiplicity in $p_A$ versus $\dim E_\lambda$; geometric ≤ algebraic
  - `eigenvalue-multiplicity` — 1 digest(s); linear-algebra — e.g. [[spielman-2025-sagt/ch01-introduction]]: repeated eigenvalue; makes eigenvectors non-unique; the drawing-based isomorphism heuristic is stated for multiplicity 1
  - `multiplicity-factor` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[jaynes-2003-probability/ch09-repetitive-p1]]: $M(n,A)$, the number of outcomes in $S^n$ on which $A$ is true; $P(A|I_0)=M/N$
- contained: `nash-equilibrium` in 3 id(s)
  - `nash-equilibrium` — 37 digest(s); decision-game-theory, economics-finance, linear-algebra, political-science — e.g. [[arthur-1994-elfarol/main]]: the mixed forecasting strategy (above 60 w.p. 0.4) is one in the pure predicting game
  - `bayesian-nash-equilibrium` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch09-bayesian]]: Nash equilibrium of the strategic game whose players are all (player, signal) pairs, with payoff (279.1)
  - `strict-nash-equilibrium` — 3 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch02-nash-p1]]: every deviation gives a strictly lower payoff to the deviator
  - `symmetric-nash-equilibrium` — 1 digest(s); decision-game-theory — e.g. [[osborne-2004-introduction/ch02-nash-p2]]: a Nash equilibrium in which all players take the same action; the steady state for one population
- contained: `nudge` in 3 id(s)
  - `nudge` — 3 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch27-law]]: later name for changes like the orange ticket
  - `nudge-choice-architecture` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/conclusion-next]]: design of defaults and reminders that changes behaviour; need not be secret
  - `nudge-unit` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[shiller-2019-narrative/ch19-future]]: think tank or government unit applying behavioral economics to nudge people away from irrational behavior without coercion; close to 200 …
  - `transparent-nudge` — 1 digest(s); economics-finance — e.g. [[thaler-2015-misbehaving/ch33-nudging]]: Thaler's definition of transparency: nothing hidden and all study results eventually public (p. 289, fn); he also says ethical nudges mus…
- contained: `p-value` in 3 id(s)

  - `bayesian-p-value` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[murphy-2023-pml2/ch03-statistics-p3]]: $\Pr(\mathrm{test}(\tilde{\mathcal D})\ge\mathrm{test}(\mathcal D)|M,\mathcal D)$
  - `p-value-critique` — 1 digest(s); information-theory — e.g. [[mackay-2003-information/ch03-more]]: a "significant" p-value need not mean substantial evidence against the null
  - `posterior-predictive-p-value` — 1 digest(s); bayesian — e.g. [[gelman-2013-bda3/ch06-model]]: $\Pr(T(y^{\rm rep},\theta)\ge T(y,\theta)\mid y)$, a posterior probability, not $\Pr$(model true)
- contained: `particle-smoother` in 3 id(s)
  - `particle-smoother` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch15-particle]]: Monte Carlo approximation of $p(\mathbf{x}_k\mid\mathbf{y}_{1:T})$ or $p(\mathbf{x}_{0:T}\mid\mathbf{y}_{1:T})$ by weighted samples
  - `rao-blackwellized-particle-smoother` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch15-particle]]: RBPF histories plus RTS smoothing of the Gaussian part
  - `reweighting-particle-smoother` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch15-particle]]: marginal smoother: new weights on filter particles, $O(TN^2)$
  - `sir-particle-smoother` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch15-particle]]: SIR with resampled full state histories, Algorithm 15.1; degenerate if $T\gg k$
- contained: `party-system` in 3 id(s)
  - `party-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch14-social-p1]]: pattern of party competition, usually described by number and size of parties; not legally fixed
  - `party-system-nationalization` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch14-social-p3]]: local and national party systems are of similar size
  - `single-party-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch14-social-p1]]: only one party legally may hold power; occurs only in dictatorships
  - `two-party-system` — 1 digest(s); political-science — e.g. [[clark-2017-principles/ch14-social-p1]]: only two major parties have a realistic chance of power

  - `iterated-posterior-linearization-filter` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch10-posterior]]: Gaussian filter with standard prediction and an IPL measurement update
  - `iterated-posterior-linearization-smoother` — 1 digest(s); control-theory — e.g. [[sarkka-2023-bayesianfiltering/ch14-general]]: IPLS: repeats SLR, Kalman filter and RTS smoother until convergence
